##### 3.5 HTTP status semantics

The DSN guide names these codes as overloaded by the API: 401, 403, 404, 406, 415, 422, 429.
Applied to the four DPAE services:

| Code | Authentication | Deposit | List returns | Download return |
|------|----------------|---------|--------------|-----------------|
| **200** | Body **is** the token | AEE returned synchronously in the body | Return list (XML) | The return document |
| **401** | — | Token missing or invalid | Token missing or invalid | Token missing or invalid |
| **403** | Account temporarily blocked after repeated failed authentications | Not registered to the service (**wait until J+1**); or concentrateur on a port ≠ 8443 | Attempting to consult returns of a flow it did not deposit | Attempting to download a return for a flow it did not deposit |
| **404** | — | — | `idFlux` unknown — **including after purge** | `idRetour` unknown — **including after purge** |
| **406** | — | Client demanded an unsupported response encoding | same | same |
| **415** | — | Request body not gzip-compressed as required | — | — |
| **422** | Invalid parameters | **Avis de rejet** — the flow failed pre-controls | Invalid parameters | — |
| **429** | — | — | Polling interval or search range violated | — |
| **400** | — | Body not compressed, or badly compressed | — | — |
| **500 / 503** | Server error / service unavailable | same | same | same |

All **[DSN→]** §9.1.1 except the 422-on-deposit meaning, which is **[DPAE]**: A2 establishes
that a flow failing pre-controls yields an *avis de rejet* (ARE) rather than an AEE, and the
DSN guide maps the avis de rejet to 422.

> **`422` on deposit is a business outcome, not a transport error.** The body carries the
> avis de rejet with its `B1-1xx-xx` reason codes (P5 §5.4). Retrying the same bytes will
> produce the same 422 forever. Route it to the reject queue, never to the retry queue.
> See P7 §7.3.

> **`404` and purge are indistinguishable.** A flow older than the retention window returns
> the same 404 as a typo'd `idFlux`. This is why P7 §7.6 requires local archival: after
> 3 months the authority cannot tell you what it told you.

> **`403` on deposit may simply mean "too early".** The DSN guide annotates it *"attendre
> J+1"* — registration propagates overnight. On the day the concentrateur registration is
> confirmed, a 403 is expected and is not a defect. Do not alert on it during the first
> 24 hours after enrolment (P6 §6.4).

##### 3.6 401 disambiguation via `WWW-Authenticate`

**[DSN→]** §9.1.6. A 401 alone is not actionable; the realm is.

| Realm string | Meaning | Correct response |
|---|---|---|
| `Basic realm="Expiration du mot de passe"` | The portal password has expired | **Human action.** Renew on Urssaf en ligne. Never retry. |
| `Basic realm="net-entreprises.fr"` | Unknown account or wrong password | **Human action.** Do not retry — repeated failures trigger the 403 lockout. |
| `DSNLogin realm="Jeton manquant ou invalide"` | Token absent or malformed | Re-authenticate once, replay once. |
| `DSNLogin realm="Expiration du jeton"` | Token expired | Re-authenticate once, replay once. **A3 says this should never occur on DPAE** — if it does, the never-expires statement is wrong and P7 §7.7 must be revisited. |
| `DSNLogin realm="Utilisateur non-inscrit au service"` | Identity valid, entitlement absent | **Human action.** Enrolment problem, not a runtime one. Escalate to `api-dpae@acoss.fr`. |

The realm strings are DSN-side literals. Their DPAE wording is **[UNVERIFIED]** — the realm
may name `mon.urssaf.fr` rather than `net-entreprises.fr`. **Match on the realm *scheme* and
on substrings (`Expiration`, `non-inscrit`), not on the full literal**, and log the raw
header so the DPAE vocabulary can be recorded on first contact (P6 §6.4).

Three of the five outcomes are **human-action, never-retry**. A client that blindly
re-authenticates on 401 will lock the account out through the 403 path. This is the single
most important error-handling rule on the channel.

##### 3.7 Retention and purge

A1 §"Contraintes d'exploitation" — **[DPAE]**, stated for the DPAE in its own words:

| Item | Window |
|------|--------|
| Return **tracking** data, via the consultation service | **13 months** (same history rule as the dashboard) |
| Returns **available for download** | **3 months**, then *"purgés et ne sont pas archivés par le SI DPAE"* |

Two distinct windows, and the shorter one is the binding constraint. After 3 months the
document itself is gone — permanently, by the authority's own statement, with no archive to
appeal to.

Set against R.1221-7 (the acknowledgement is the *proof* of declaration) and R.1221-12 (a
labour inspector may demand it), the conclusion is not optional:

> **Every return must be downloaded and stored on our side, well inside 3 months.**
> The authority's retention is not our archive. See P7 §7.6.

Note also R.1221-16: the receiving body keeps the declaration data itself for **six months**.
That is a *third* window and it governs the declaration, not the returns.

##### 3.8 TLS and certificate authorities

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| HTTPS on all four services | **[DPAE]** — every published URL is `https://` |
| Client certificate / mTLS | Not required; not mentioned anywhere |
| Accepted CAs for `mon.urssaf.fr` and `*.dpae-edi.urssaf.fr` | **[UNVERIFIED]** — no DPAE artefact publishes a chain, and the DSN guide's CA annex covers `*.net-entreprises.fr` and partner hosts only |
| Minimum TLS version | **[UNVERIFIED]** — no DPAE statement |

Assume TLS 1.2 minimum and publicly-trusted CAs; verify at enrolment (P2 §2.8 item 5) or at
first authorised connectivity test (P6 §6.4). **Do not disable strict TLS verification** —
the DSN guide offers that shortcut for its own hosts, and it is the wrong trade on a channel
whose authentication body carries a portal password in clear.

##### 3.9 Polling contract

**[DSN→]** §8.3, and it applies to the **list** service only. The guide is explicit that
*"il n'y a en revanche pas de restriction d'utilisation des services de dépôt et de
téléchargement"*.

| Rule | Mechanism |
|------|-----------|
| Minimum interval between two searches | Read from the `Expires` header of the **last HTTP 200**. Only 200s carry it and only 200s advance the server-side counter — after an error you may retry immediately. |
| Maximum search-range width | Read from `Accept-Ranges: minutes=0-N` on the response. |
| Violation | `429 Too Many Requests` |

The interval is a server-side parameter, adjustable at any time. **Never hard-code it.**
Read `Expires`, honour it, and treat a 429 as "I ignored a header I was given", not as a
capacity problem.

Because per-declarant search is unconfirmed for the DPAE (§3.1), polling in practice means
looping `lister-retours-flux` over recently-deposited `idFlux` values. That is a bounded set
— one entry per deposit — so the interval, not the range, is the operative constraint. See
P7 §7.2 for the schedule.

##### 3.10 Chunked transfer

**[DSN→]** §7.3. `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` is optional on requests (client's choice) and
at the server's discretion on responses. The `TE` header has no effect. **Every client must
be able to consume a chunked response.** Relevant here because a large return list or a
multi-hire AR can arrive chunked.

##### 3.11 Reference implementation

The GIP-MDS public reference client (`ArchitectureDSN/dsn-api`, already acquired under the
NETENT-DSN source set) implements `services/auth`, `services/depot`, `services/recherche`
and `services/telechargement` — the same four families, against the DSN hosts.

It is **usable as a structural reference for the DPAE** precisely because A1 delegates the
machine-mode interface to the DSN. It is **not** usable as a configuration source: hosts,
the `/authentifier_dpae` path shape, the port-8443 rule and the payload are all different.
Read it to see the call sequence; take every constant from §3.2.


### URSSAF-DPAE / `P4-outbound-payload-format120.md`

#### P4 — Outbound Payload Specification (format 120 / `FR_DUE_Upload`)
**FR · URSSAF-DPAE**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · 2 gated artefacts (XML schema, `C_URSSAF` table)
Source basis: A4 (*Dessin d'enregistrement*, format 120, v5.4), A5 (v5.3), A2 (control
tables), A1 (XML instance + deposit contract), A3 (deposit limits and test mode), A6 (SPST
reference), Code du travail R.1221-1

> **Scope of this phase.** This is a **structural** specification: the record geometry, the
> two payload variants, the validation contract and the deposit rules. It gives the full
> 990-byte layout because that layout *is* the structure — there are only 24 real fields.
> Business mapping of payroll data onto those fields belongs to
> `FR-compliance/05-data-dictionary/`.

---

##### 4.1 Two payload variants — pick the flat one

A1 §2.1.1: *"Les formats DPAE XML et DPAE à plat sont acceptés."* Both are equally valid on
the API. They are not equally buildable.

| | **Format 120 flat (TXT)** | **`FR_DUE_Upload` XML** |
|---|---|---|
| Layout published? | **Yes** — A4 and A5 give every position | **Partially.** A1 shows one instance; **no XSD is published** |
| Validation offline | Positional + A2's control tables | Impossible without the schema |
| Field vocabulary | Numeric (`C_TYPE_CONTRAT` = `1`/`2`/`3`) | Mnemonic (`FR_Contract.Nature.Code` = `CDI`) — **different value domain** |
| Test indicator | `C_VERSION` = `TST` / `120` | `FR_DUE_Upload.Test.Indicator` = `1` / `120` |
| Corroboration | Two independent witnesses (A4, A5) plus A2's control tables keyed to the same field names | One example in one document |

**Recommendation: build format 120 flat.** It is the variant the authority actually
specifies, the variant its control tables are written against, and the variant whose error
codes (§4.6) are documented field by field. The XML variant is buildable only by copying a
single example, and the two variants **do not share a value domain** — a build that treats
them as serialisations of one model will get contract type wrong.

If the XML variant is ever required, the schema is a hard prerequisite:

> `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` — **`DUE_Upload.xsd`.** A1 §2.1.1 shows an `FR_DUE_Upload` instance
> declaring six `repxml.org` namespaces (`DataTypes`, `Organization`, `PostalAddress`,
> `Person_Identity`, `PhoneAddress`) plus a UBL core-component-types namespace, and gives no
> schema URL. No public URL was found. Request from `api-dpae@acoss.fr` at enrolment.

##### 4.2 Format identity

| Property | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Norm version | **`120`** — carried in the record itself as `C_VERSION` | A4, A10 |
| Prior versions | **`100` and `110` are rejected.** Not deprecated — rejected. | A10 |
| Record length | **990 bytes including the terminating line break** | A5, A2 |
| Payload length | **989** characters + `LF` | A4 total row |
| Line terminator | `LF` — `chr(10)`, `\n`, `0x0A`. **Not CRLF.** | A4, A5 |
| Field justification | Left-aligned, **blank-padded** | A4, A5 |
| Case | **Mandatory fields must be UPPERCASE** | A4, A5 |
| Encoding | **ISO-8859-1** (see note) | inferred — see below |
| Multi-record | Yes. Many hires per file; A2 recommends grouping one employer's DPAE consecutively. | A2, A3 |

> **On encoding.** No artefact states the flat file's character encoding in words. It is
> nonetheless determined: A2's permitted-character lists for `L_RAISON_SOC_1`,
> `L_BUR_DIST_EMP` and `L_ADR_EMP_*` include `é è ê ë â à ä ö ô û ü î ï ç ° ²`, all of which
> are single bytes in ISO-8859-1 and none of which is ASCII; and A1 declares
> `encoding="ISO-8859-1"` on the XML variant. **Emit ISO-8859-1. Do not emit UTF-8** — a
> two-byte `é` would shift every subsequent field and break the 989-character contract.
> Marked as **derived, not quoted.**

> `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` — **the API-channel dessin d'enregistrement.** A1 cites *"Dessin
> d'enregistrement DPAE, V3.5"* as reference [1]. What is publicly obtainable is v5.3 (A5,
> titled for the **EMAIL** channel) and v5.4 (A4, titled for the **CFT** channel) — both
> **later** revisions, both titled for channels that have since closed. §4.3 marks the one
> region where the two differ, which is where API-specific risk concentrates.

##### 4.3 Record layout — all 989 characters

From A4 (v5.4). Positions are 1-based and inclusive. `Réservé` regions must be **blanks**.

###### Catégorie I — ENTETE (1–77)

| Field | Start | End | Len | Content |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L_IDENT_EMT` | 1 | 14 | 14 | Sender/batch identifier. **Must not begin with `UP`** (reserved). Must contain at least one unaccented letter. Uppercase letters and digits only, no embedded blanks. One distinct value per sending site. |
| `C_APPLICATION` | 15 | 18 | 4 | Application to invoke. **Channel-specific** — `"DUE "` on the EMAIL variant, `"CFT"` on the CFT variant. **See the warning below.** |
| *Réservé* | 19 | 34 | 16 | 16 blanks |
| `C_ADR_RET_AR` | 35 | 64 | 30 | Return address for the AR to the employer |
| `N_LG_MESS` | 65 | 72 | 8 | Record length **excluding** the 77-character header → **`912`** when the record is fully populated or blank-padded |
| `C_VERSION` | 73 | 75 | 3 | **`120`** for a real filing, **`TST`** for a test. Anything else → batch error `11` |
| `C_RET_AR_LOT` | 76 | 77 | 2 | Batch-level return code. **Set by the system**, not by us. `00` = nominal |

> **`C_APPLICATION` is the one field this runbook cannot state with certainty for the API
> channel.** A5 (EMAIL) requires `"DUE "`; A4 (CFT) requires `"CFT"`. Neither A1, A2 nor A3
> names the API value. Three readings are possible — a third literal, one of the two
> existing values, or an ignored field. **UNVERIFIED.** Resolve it in the enrolment email
> (P2 §2.8 item 5) and confirm it in the first `TST` deposit (P6 §6.5 case T-01) before any
> real filing. A wrong value here plausibly fails the whole flow at pre-control, which is a
> loud and early failure — the cheap kind.

###### Catégorie II — DPAE (78–431)

| Field | Start | End | Len | Content | R.1221-1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MATRICULE` | 78 | 121 | 44 | Employee payroll number. **Free text, not validated.** | — |
| `D_CREATION` | 122 | 129 | 8 | Record creation date, `JJMMAAAA` | — |
| `H_CREATION` | 130 | 133 | 4 | Record creation time, `HHMM` | — |
| `C_URSSAF` | 134 | 136 | 3 | Receiving URSSAF code. **Must appear in Annexe II.1** — §4.5 | — |
| `N_SIRET` | 137 | 150 | 14 | Employer SIRET | 1° |
| *Réservé* | 151 | 151 | 1 | 1 blank | |
| `L_RAISON_SOC_1` | 152 | 183 | 32 | Company name, line 1 | 1° |
| *Réservé* | 184 | 187 | 4 | 4 blanks | |
| `L_ADR_EMP_1` | 188 | 219 | 32 | Establishment address, line 1 | 1° |
| `L_ADR_EMP_2` | 220 | 251 | 32 | Establishment address, line 2 (optional) | 1° |
| `C_POSTAL_EMP` | 252 | 256 | 5 | Postcode, 5 digits, **`00000` invalid** | 1° |
| `L_BUR_DIST_EMP` | 257 | 283 | 27 | Town | 1° |
| `L_NOM_PATRO_SAL` | 284 | 315 | 32 | Employee birth surname | 2° |
| `L_PRENOMS_SAL` | 316 | 347 | 32 | Employee first name(s) | 2° |
| `N_SECU_SOC` | 348 | 360 | 13 | NIR. **13 or 15 characters** (15 = with key). Accepts letters for Corsica `2A`/`2B` | 2° |
| `D_NAISSANCE_SAL` | 361 | 368 | 8 | Date of birth, `JJMMAAAA`, must be < today | 2° |
| `L_LIEU_NAISS_SAL` | 369 | 392 | 24 | Place of birth (commune or country) | 2° |
| *Réservé* | 393 | 412 | 20 | 20 blanks | |
| `D_EMBAUCHE` | 413 | 420 | 8 | **Hire date**, `JJMMAAAA` | 3° |
| `H_EMBAUCHE` | 421 | 424 | 4 | **Hire time**, `HHMM` | 3° |
| `R_DOSSIER` | 425 | 429 | 5 | **Case reference — returned, not sent.** See §4.9 | — |
| `C_RETOUR_AR` | 430 | 431 | 2 | **Per-declaration return code — returned, not sent.** See §4.9 | — |

###### Catégorie III — EMPLOYEUR (432–698)

| Field | Start | End | Len | Content |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| *Réservé* | 432 | 509 | 78 | 78 blanks (obsolete fields removed at v5.4) |
| `L_RAISON_SOC_2` | 510 | 541 | 32 | Company name, line 2 (optional) |
| `N_TEL_EMP` | 542 | 552 | 11 | Telephone (optional; 10 digits when present) |
| `N_FAX_EMP` | 553 | 659 | 107 | **107 blanks** — obsolete, blank-fill |
| `C_ORIG_SAISIE` | 660 | 660 | 1 | **Constant `E`** |
| `C_NAF_NN` | 661 | 665 | 5 | APE/NAF code, 2008 nomenclature |
| *Réservé* | 666 | 698 | 33 | 33 blanks |

###### Catégorie IV — SALARIE (699–864)

| Field | Start | End | Len | Content |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L_NOM_EPX_SAL` | 699 | 730 | 32 | Married name (optional) |
| `C_SEXE_SAL` | 731 | 731 | 1 | **`M` or `F`.** Mandatory since v5.4 | 2° |
| *Réservé* | 732 | 827 | 96 | 96 blanks |
| `C_DEPT_NAISS_SAL` | 828 | 829 | 2 | **Department of birth.** Mandatory | 2° |
| *Réservé* | 830 | 864 | 35 | 35 blanks |

###### Catégorie V — CONTRAT (865–947)

| Field | Start | End | Len | Content | R.1221-1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C_TYPE_CONTRAT` | 865 | 865 | 1 | **`1` = CDD · `2` = CDI · `3` = CTT.** Mandatory since v5.4 | 4° |
| `D_FIN_CDD` | 866 | 873 | 8 | CDD end date, `JJMMAAAA`. Mandatory for CDD. Time is system-set to `23:59:59` | 4° |
| *Réservé* | 874 | 884 | 11 | 11 blanks |
| `N_JJ_ESSAI` | 885 | 887 | 3 | Trial period in days, digits only | 4° |
| *Réservé* | 888 | 937 | 50 | 50 blanks |
| `C_MT_DCL` | 938 | 947 | 10 | **Occupational health service code**, `MT` + 3 digits (e.g. `MT542`), or `MT999` for an in-house service. Mandatory **except** for `C_TYPE_CONTRAT = 3` (CTT). Reference table: §4.5 | 1° |

###### Catégorie VI — FORMALITES (948–989) — system-set, send blanks

| Field | Start | End | Len | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| *Réservé* | 948 | 970 | 23 | 23 blanks |
| `I_CERTIF` | 971 | 971 | 1 | DADS pre-establishment data → **CRAM** |
| *Réservé* | 972 | 973 | 2 | 2 blanks |
| `I_IMMA` | 974 | 974 | 1 | Registration request for an employee with no NIR → **CPAM** |
| `I_MT` | 975 | 975 | 1 | Occupational health service notified → **SPST** |
| `I_PMF5` | 976 | 976 | 1 | First hire in the establishment → **CFE-URSSAF** |
| *Réservé* | 977 | 989 | 13 | 13 blanks |

> **Catégorie VI is an output channel embedded in the input record.** All four flags are
> *"Positionné par le système"* (A4). Since format 120 the declarant **can no longer steer
> them**: A10 states *"Vous ne pouvez plus piloter vos formalités : en fonction des données
> saisies, les formalités seront transmises aux partenaires (ex : l'absence de NNI génère
> une demande d'immatriculation à la CPAM)."* Send blanks; **read them back off the AR.**
> They tell you which of the four downstream bodies were engaged.

###### Arithmetic check

`977 + 13 − 1 = 989`. `989 + 1 (LF) = 990`. `989 − 77 (header) = 912 = N_LG_MESS`. All three
tie out against A2's pre-control (*"Pour les fichiers txt : la longueur des DPAE est de
990"*) and A4's own total row. **Assert all three at build time** — P6 §6.3.

###### Where A4 and A5 differ

One region only: positions **78–121**. A5 (v5.3) has three `LIBRE` zones there
(78–92, 93–107, 108–121), uncontrolled and available for declarant-internal use, one of
which serves as the AR email subject. A4 (v5.4) replaces all three with a single
44-character `MATRICULE`. **A4 is the later revision and is the build target.** If a `TST`
deposit rejects on this region, A5's tripartite reading is the fallback hypothesis.

##### 4.4 Mapping to the legal content requirement

R.1221-1 fixes what a DPAE must contain. The format-120 record covers it exactly — a useful
completeness check that does not depend on any technical document:

| R.1221-1 | Fields |
|---|---|
| 1° Employer name, APE code, address, SIRET, and the occupational health service | `L_RAISON_SOC_1/2`, `C_NAF_NN`, `L_ADR_EMP_1/2` + `C_POSTAL_EMP` + `L_BUR_DIST_EMP`, `N_SIRET`, `C_MT_DCL` |
| 2° Employee surname, first names, sex, date and place of birth, and NIR if already registered | `L_NOM_PATRO_SAL`, `L_PRENOMS_SAL`, `C_SEXE_SAL`, `D_NAISSANCE_SAL`, `L_LIEU_NAISS_SAL` + `C_DEPT_NAISS_SAL`, `N_SECU_SOC` |
| 3° Date and time of hire | `D_EMBAUCHE`, `H_EMBAUCHE` |
| 4° Nature and duration of contract, and trial period for CDI and for CDD longer than six months | `C_TYPE_CONTRAT`, `D_FIN_CDD`, `N_JJ_ESSAI` |

**Every legally mandatory element has exactly one field. There is no optional legal
content.** The record's genuinely optional fields — `MATRICULE`, `L_RAISON_SOC_2`,
`L_ADR_EMP_2`, `N_TEL_EMP`, `L_NOM_EPX_SAL` — carry nothing R.1221-1 asks for.

##### 4.5 Reference tables

| Field | Table | Availability |
|---|---|---|
| `C_MT_DCL` | **SPST / occupational health services.** URSSAF open data, dataset `sst_dpae` — 409 rows, columns `mt_code_ur`, `mt_lib_court` (the `MTnnn` value), `mt_lib`, address block. Acquired as **A6**. `MT999` = in-house service; `MT01` also appears as an internal-medicine marker. | **Available and machine-consumable.** Refresh from open data, do not hard-code. |
| `C_NAF_NN` | APE/NAF 2008 nomenclature (INSEE). Must be 4 or 5 characters and referenced in the DPAE system. **`7820Z` is mandatory when `C_TYPE_CONTRAT = 3` (CTT)** | Standard INSEE reference |
| `C_URSSAF` | **Annexe II.1** — the list of receiving URSSAF codes | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` — see below |

> `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` — **the `C_URSSAF` code table.** A4/A5 bind error code `02` to
> *"Code URSSAF invalide — n'appartenant pas à la liste ANNEXE II.1"*. URSSAF publishes the
> table at `fichierdirect.declaration.urssaf.fr/TablesReference.htm`, linked from A12; **that
> host would not accept a connection from this environment.** A6 yields the **26** UR codes
> that have at least one SPST attached (`117, 200, 217, 227, 247, 267, 287, 317, 417, 427,
> 437, 527, 537, 547, 727, 737, 747, 827, 837, 917, 937, 971, 972, 973, 974, 976`) — a
> **subset**, not the list, and not safe to validate against. Retrieve the full table before
> go-live; note that A4's revision history records repeated URSSAF mergers (`761+762+763=760`,
> `592+593+594=590`, …), so a stale copy silently produces error `02`.

##### 4.6 Validation contract

A2 gives the complete control set, in the order it is applied. Enforce as much of it as
possible **before** deposit — every rule below is a cheap local check that avoids a
round-trip and, for the business controls, a rejected hire.

###### Layer 1 — pre-controls (flow level)

| Rule | Failure |
|---|---|
| Flow is a single non-empty file, under the configured size limit | `B1-104-06` / `B1-104-07` |
| **API: archives are not accepted** (A1). The gz/tar/zip rules in A2 apply to the *portal upload* path | — |
| Extension is text or XML | `B1-105-10`…`B1-105-15` (six distinct codes, one label — URSSAF distinguishes them internally) |
| `C_VERSION` ∈ {`120`, `TST`} | batch code `11` |
| TXT record length is **990** | batch code `15` |

Codes `B1-104-02`…`B1-104-05` concern archive size, emptiness, unreadability and nesting
depth — **portal path only**.

**Failing a pre-control rejects the entire flow** and returns an *avis de rejet* (ARE)
instead of an AEE. There is no partial acceptance at this layer.

###### Layer 2 — declarant SIRET control

| Rule | Failure |
|---|---|
| Every employer SIRET in the flow exists in the enterprise/establishment referential with no cessation date | `B1-201-01` *Siret inconnu* |
| SIRET not struck off | `B1-208-01` *Siret radié au JJ/MM/AAAA* |

A2 adds a nuance worth knowing: *"Le siret employeur rattaché à un compte Urssaf radié ou
suspendu est susceptible de transmettre des DPAE."* A suspended contribution account does
**not** block filing.

###### Layer 3 — norm controls (per declaration)

Employer data:

| Field | Rule | Code |
|---|---|---|
| `N_SIRET` | Mandatory | `02` |
| `C_URSSAF` | Mandatory · length ≥ 4 rejected · must be in the URSSAF table | `02` |
| `C_NAF_NN` | Mandatory · length 4 or 5 · must be referenced in the DPAE system | `03` |
| `L_RAISON_SOC_1` | Mandatory · ≤ 32 · charset `a-zA-Z0-9éèêë âàä öô ûü îï ç°² !#$%&'()*+,-./:;<=>?@` | `05` |
| `L_ADR_EMP_1` | Mandatory · ≤ 32 · same charset plus `ù` | `06` |
| `C_POSTAL_EMP` | Mandatory · digits only · exactly 5 · **`00000` invalid** | `07` |
| `L_BUR_DIST_EMP` | Mandatory · ≤ 32 · same charset | `08` |
| `L_RAISON_SOC_2`, `L_ADR_EMP_2`, `N_TEL_EMP` | Optional; length and charset still checked | `99` |

Employee data:

| Field | Rule | Code |
|---|---|---|
| `L_NOM_PATRO_SAL` | Mandatory · ≤ 32 · charset **`A-Z' .&-` only** | `31` |
| `L_PRENOMS_SAL` | Mandatory · ≤ 32 · charset **`A-Z' .&-` only** | `32` |
| `D_NAISSANCE_SAL` | Mandatory · valid · strictly before today | `33` |
| `L_LIEU_NAISS_SAL` | Mandatory · ≤ 32 · charset `A-Z0-9 .'-` | `34` |
| `N_SECU_SOC` | 13 or 15 characters · consistent with sex and date of birth · **if 15, the key must verify** · letters permitted only for `2A`/`2B` | `38` |
| `C_DEPT_NAISS_SAL` | Mandatory | `47` |
| `C_SEXE_SAL` | `M` or `F`, mandatory | `43` |

> **Employee name fields are uppercase, unaccented, and digit-free.** The permitted set is
> `A-Z`, apostrophe, space, `.`, `&`, `-`. Employer fields tolerate accents and digits;
> employee name fields do not. A payroll master holding `Zoé Muñoz-O'Brien` must be
> **uppercased and transliterated** to `ZOE MUNOZ-O'BRIEN` before emission. This is a real
> transformation with real edge cases (`ß`, `Æ`, `Ø`, CJK), and it is the most likely source
> of avoidable code-31/32 rejections. Specify it once, in the Data Dictionary, and reuse.

Contract data:

| Field | Rule | Code |
|---|---|---|
| `D_EMBAUCHE` | Mandatory · valid · **not more than one year in the past nor one year in the future** | `35` |
| `H_EMBAUCHE` | `0 ≤ HH ≤ 23`, `0 ≤ MM ≤ 59` | `35` |
| `C_MT_DCL` | Mandatory **except** when `C_TYPE_CONTRAT = 3` · must be in the SPST reference or be an internal service (`MT01` / `MT999`) | `50` |
| `N_JJ_ESSAI` | ≤ 3 characters · digits only | `99` |
| `C_TYPE_CONTRAT` | Mandatory · ∈ {`1`, `2`, `3`} | `44` |
| CTT ⇒ `C_NAF_NN` | If `C_TYPE_CONTRAT = 3` then `C_NAF_NN` **must be `7820Z`** | `45` |
| `D_FIN_CDD` | Valid date · **≥ `D_EMBAUCHE`** · mandatory for CDD | `46` |

> **The ±1-year window on `D_EMBAUCHE` is narrower than it looks.** R.1221-4 allows filing at
> most 8 days ahead; the format allows a year either way. The format is *permissive* here and
> the **law is the binding constraint** — filing 30 days ahead passes code 35 and breaches
> R.1221-4. Enforce the 8-day rule in our own pre-flight (P6 §6.3, assertion 6); the channel
> will not enforce it for us.

###### Layer 4 — business controls (bloc 2, after conformity certification)

A2: *"À ce jour, seule la vérification de l'absence de doublon est contrôlée d'un point de
vue métier."* One rule:

| Rule | Code |
|---|---|
| No duplicate DPAE. The duplicate key is **surname + first name + date of birth + hire date + hire time + SIRET** (A4, code 98) | `98` — the DPAE is **rejected** |

A2 adds: *"si les éléments fournis dans la DPAE initiale conviennent, aucune action n'est à
opérer."* Code 98 usually means *already filed*, not *failed*. Handle it as an idempotency
signal, not an error — P5 §5.5.

##### 4.7 Deposit rules

| Rule | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Method / resource | `POST /deposer-dsn/1.0/` | A1 |
| Body | The flow as generated, gzip-compressed | A1 |
| `Content-Type` | `text/plain` | A1 |
| **Archives** | **Not accepted** | A1, verbatim |
| Multiple hires per file | **Yes**, for one or more establishments | A3, A2 |
| Maximum deposit size | **100 MB** | A3 |
| Ordering recommendation | Group one employer's DPAE consecutively in a multi-employer file | A2 |
| Partial failure | **Per declaration.** *"Seule la DPAE en anomalie est rejetée."* | A3 |
| Frequency | No constraint. *"Le mode API permet une utilisation au fil de l'eau."* | A3 |

At 990 bytes per record, the 100 MB ceiling is ~105 000 declarations per deposit — not a
practical constraint. A2's separate recommendation to cap a *day's* batch at 50 000
declarations was written for CFT and does not bind the API.

> **This is the opposite of the DSN rule, and the difference is deliberate.** The DSN accepts
> only unitary deposit (one `S20` block per call). The DPAE accepts bulk and is designed for
> it — A2 describes the API's purpose as *"la gestion d'un très grand nombre de dépôts"*. An
> engineer carrying DSN habits across will build one-call-per-hire and needlessly multiply
> traffic; an engineer carrying DPAE habits back will break the DSN. See INTERLINKAGES §2.

##### 4.8 There is no correction model

A2 is explicit on all three points:

- *"la fonction « annule et remplace » n'est pas nécessaire pour la gestion d'un flux DPAE EDI"*
- *"Les contrôles de cinématique ne sont pas justifiés pour la DPAE"* — no declared period, no
  cancel-and-replace, so nothing to sequence
- duplicates are handled in bloc 2 by rejection with code `98`

Consequences for the build:

1. **A registered DPAE cannot be amended through this channel.** There is no correction
   payload, no replacement nature, no annulment. Re-sending a corrected record for the same
   employee and hire datetime yields code `98`.
2. **Corrections are an out-of-band process**, through the employer's Urssaf or the portal.
   A hire whose data was wrong is a **support case, not a retry**.
3. **Therefore the pre-flight gate matters more here than on any other channel.** A DSN error
   is corrected next month; a DPAE error is corrected by telephone. P6 §6.3 is not
   ceremonial.
4. Changing the *hire datetime* changes the duplicate key — so a rescheduled start produces a
   **second registered DPAE**, not an amendment. Whether that is correct is a business
   question, not a technical one. Flag it to the client.

##### 4.9 Two fields you send blank and read back

`R_DOSSIER` (425–429) and `C_RETOUR_AR` (430–431) sit inside the record but are **populated
by URSSAF on the AR**, not by us:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `R_DOSSIER` | The reference under which the DPAE is registered. A2 and A4 both state: *"Une DPAE correctement enregistrée renvoie obligatoirement une référence dossier dans le champ `R_DOSSIER`."* |
| `C_RETOUR_AR` | Per-declaration outcome. `00` = registered · `98` = already exists · `99` = accepted with errors on optional fields (a `R_DOSSIER` **is** issued) · anything else = the norm-control code from §4.6 |

> **`R_DOSSIER` is the proof of registration and the reconciliation key.** Not HTTP 200; not
> the AEE; not the certificat de conformité. A populated `R_DOSSIER` on the AR. Persist it
> against the employee and the hire event, and treat its absence as *not filed*. See
> P5 §5.3, P7 §7.6.

> **`99` is a pass, not a failure.** A2: *"ce code d'erreur signifie que la DPAE a été prise
> en compte mais qu'elle contenait des erreurs sur un ou plusieurs champs facultatifs. Une
> référence dossier est donc renseignée."* Alerting on `99` as an error will generate noise
> and, worse, may trigger a re-send that returns `98`. Log it, surface it as a data-quality
> item, do not re-file.

##### 4.10 Test mode

A3 answers *"Comment réaliser un dépôt de test ?"* directly: there is no test environment.
Deposit a test declaration **on the production service** from a real client account, with:

| Variant | Test | Real |
|---|---|---|
| Flat | `C_VERSION` = **`TST`** | `C_VERSION` = **`120`** |
| XML | `FR_DUE_Upload.Test.Indicator` = **`1`** | `= 120` |

Note the asymmetry: the flat test marker is `TST`, the XML test marker is `1`, and **`120` is
the real-mode value in both**. A build that pairs `1`/`120` on the flat variant will file a
real DPAE while believing it is testing.

What a `TST` deposit yields is constrained — A1's return-availability matrix shows only the
AEE/avis de rejet (10) and the certificat de conformité / bilan d'anomalies (11) exist in
`TEST`; the BIS (20) and the AR (41) are `RÉEL` only. **So a test deposit can never return an
`R_DOSSIER`.** Test proves the record parses and passes norm controls; it cannot prove
registration. See P5 §5.2 and P6 §6.5.

##### 4.11 Outbound build checklist

| # | Item |
|---|---|
| 1 | Build the **flat format 120** variant (§4.1). Do not build both. |
| 2 | Emit **ISO-8859-1**, `LF` line endings, exactly **990 bytes per record** (§4.2) |
| 3 | Assert `989 + LF`, `N_LG_MESS = 912`, and header = 77 at emit time (§4.3) |
| 4 | Resolve `C_APPLICATION` for the API channel before any real filing (§4.3) |
| 5 | Uppercase + transliterate employee name fields to `A-Z' .&-` (§4.6) |
| 6 | Blank-fill every `Réservé` region and all of Catégorie VI (§4.3) |
| 7 | Load `C_MT_DCL` from open data (A6); **do not hard-code** (§4.5) |
| 8 | Obtain the full `C_URSSAF` table before go-live (§4.5) |
| 9 | Enforce **R.1221-4's 8-day window** locally — the format will not (§4.6) |
| 10 | Enforce the CTT ⇒ `7820Z` and CDD ⇒ `D_FIN_CDD ≥ D_EMBAUCHE` cross-field rules (§4.6) |
| 11 | Compute the duplicate key locally and suppress re-sends before they earn a `98` (§4.6) |
| 12 | gzip the body; `Content-Type: text/plain`; **never** send an archive (§4.7) |
| 13 | Treat `C_VERSION` as a deployment-controlled value, never a per-record one (§4.10) |
| 14 | Persist `idFlux` at deposit and `R_DOSSIER` at AR (§4.9, P3 §3.1) |


### URSSAF-DPAE / `P5-inbound-returns.md`

#### P5 — Inbound Returns Specification
**FR · URSSAF-DPAE**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · return payload layouts gated
Source basis: A1 (return-availability matrix, retention), A3 (return scenarios, formats,
call sequence), A2 (control outcomes, error-code catalogue, BIS semantics), A4 (return-code
lists), Code du travail R.1221-7 / R.1221-9 / R.1221-12

---

##### 5.1 Four return types, two format families

A1's *Disponibilité des retours par environnements* matrix, p. 4. This table is reproduced
from a `pdftotext -lineprinter` extraction because the merged cells in the source render
unusably under `-layout`; the row/column bindings below are unambiguous in that rendering.

| Return | Type | Code | Origin | `TEST` | `RÉEL` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **AEE** — accusé d'enregistrement électronique · **ARE** — avis de rejet | `AR` | **10** | SI DPAE | ✔ | ✔ |
| **CCO** — certificat de conformité · **BAN** — bilan d'anomalies | `CR` | **11** | SI DPAE | ✔ | ✔ |
| **BIS** — bilan d'identification des salariés | `~AR` | **20** | SI DPAE | — | ✔ |
| **AR** — accusé de réception, a.k.a. *certificat de conformité des DPAE* | `CR` | **41** | SI DPAE | — | ✔ |

Note that 10 and 11 each cover a **success/failure pair** in one code — the code tells you
which *stage* reported, not whether it passed. Status is carried inside the document.

Format provenance, from A3 verbatim:

> *"Les formats des retours 10 et 11 sont identiques à ceux délivrés en DSN. Les retours 20 et
> 41 sont spécifiques à la DPAE et restent inchangés par rapport à ceux proposés
> postérieurement à la mise en place de l'API DPAE."*

| Codes | Format family | Reusable from the DSN build? |
|---|---|---|
| **10, 11** | DSN harmonised "bilans" format (A1 reference [3]: `dsn_bilans_v01r06.xsd`) | **Yes.** Same parser as NETENT-DSN P5. |
| **20, 41** | DPAE-proprietary. Format 120 for the AR; "Doc v1.3" for the BIS (A2, Annexe 2) | **No.** Separate parsers. |

**This is the single most valuable reuse fact on the channel** — half the return surface is
already built if the DSN integration exists. It is also the sharpest trap: the other half is
a fixed-width 990-byte record and a document format neither published nor versioned in any
artefact we could obtain.

##### 5.2 Retrieval sequence

A3 gives the whole flow with literal URLs. Four steps, three of them inbound:

```
1. POST https://mon.urssaf.fr/authentifier_dpae                        → token
2. POST https://depot.dpae-edi.urssaf.fr/deposer-dsn/1.0/              → AEE (or ARE) + idFlux
3. GET  https://consultation.dpae-edi.urssaf.fr/lister-retours-flux/1.0/{idFlux}
                                                                      → list of available returns
                                                                        (repeatable — A3: "Le listage
                                                                         peut être répété")
4. GET  https://telechargement.dpae-edi.urssaf.fr/telecharger-retour/1.0/{idFlux}/{idRetour}
                                                                      → the return document
```

Concentrateurs: port **8443** on steps 2–4, not on step 1 (P3 §3.2).

**Step 4's URL must not be constructed.** A3: *"L'URL complète peut être récupérée dans la
réponse à la requête précédente."* A1 v1.1's stated change was precisely the addition of the
`<url>` element to the return lists. Take the URL from the list response — this is a
hypermedia contract, and the host in that URL may differ from the base host.

###### The list response

No DPAE artefact reproduces it. Under A1's delegation to the DSN guide, expect a `<retours>`
document containing one `<flux>` per flow, each with `<retour>` elements carrying
`publication`, `production`, `nature` (the 10/11/20/41 code), `statut`, `id` and `url`. An
empty result is `200 OK` with an empty `<retours></retours>`, **not** a 404 — a 404 means the
`idFlux` is unknown *or purged* (P3 §3.5).

**[DSN→], and the `nature` values are the DPAE's own.** Do not assume the DSN's `statut`
vocabulary transfers verbatim; log raw values on first contact (P6 §6.4).

###### Timing

| Return | When |
|---|---|
| **10** (AEE/ARE) | **Synchronous** — in the deposit response body |
| **11** (CCO/BAN) | Asynchronous, after norm controls |
| **41** (AR) | Asynchronous, after business processing in bloc 2 |
| **20** (BIS) | **J+1 after the employee's hire date** — A2: *"Le Bis est mis à disposition à J+1 de la date d'embauche du salarié."* |

The BIS timing is unusual and drives the polling schedule: it is keyed to the **hire date**,
not the deposit date. A DPAE filed 8 days ahead produces a BIS 9 days after deposit. A poller
that stops watching a flow once the AR arrives will never see the BIS. See P7 §7.2.

##### 5.3 The acceptance chain — HTTP 200 is not a filing

Four gates, in order. Each can pass while the next fails.

| # | Gate | Evidence of passing | Evidence of failing |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Transport | HTTP `200` | `401`/`403`/`415`/`5xx` |
| 2 | Pre-controls | **AEE** (code 10) with an `IdFluxDPAE` | **ARE** — avis de rejet, HTTP `422`, with `B1-1xx-xx` reasons |
| 3 | Norm controls | **Certificat de conformité** (code 11) | **Bilan d'anomalies** (code 11) with per-field codes |
| 4 | Business processing | **AR** (code 41) with a populated **`R_DOSSIER`** and `C_RETOUR_AR` = `00` or `99` | `C_RETOUR_AR` = `98` (duplicate) or a norm code |

> **Only gate 4 means the hire is declared.** A2 and A4 both state it: *"Une DPAE
> correctement enregistrée renvoie obligatoirement une référence dossier dans le champ
> `R_DOSSIER`."* R.1221-7 makes the acknowledgement the *proof* of declaration; R.1221-12
> lets a labour inspector demand it. A build that reports "filed" at gate 1 will report
> success for hires that were never registered — and will do so on a **statutory, pre-hire,
> per-employee** obligation where the failure is discovered by an inspector.

A1 is careful about what the AEE actually attests, and it is worth quoting because it is
narrower than the name suggests. Per A2, the AEE certifies only:

- that the flow was received,
- that it passed the pre-controls and can be processed,
- that it has a unique `IdFluxDPAE`.

Nothing about the declarations inside it.

###### AEE and ARE contents

A2 enumerates the minimum content of each — useful because these are the two documents you
receive synchronously and must parse first:

| AEE contains | ARE contains |
|---|---|
| `IdFluxDPAE` | `IdFluxDPAE` |
| Flow name | Flow name |
| Archive name, if part of one | Archive name, if part of one |
| Declarant reference (the registered triplet SIRET + nom + prénom) | Declarant reference |
| AEE generation timestamp | **Rejection reason(s)** |

A2 also states the mutual exclusion plainly: *"il n'est pas possible d'avoir à la fois
l'accusé d'enregistrement électronique et l'avis de rejet pour un flux."* One or the other,
never both.

##### 5.4 Return catalogue

###### Flow-level rejection codes (in the ARE)

From A2, applied at pre-control. `XXXXXX` is the file or archive name.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `B1-104-02` | Archive too large |
| `B1-104-03` | Archive of zero size |
| `B1-104-04` | Archive unusable |
| `B1-104-05` | More than one level of archive nesting |
| `B1-104-06` | File of zero size |
| `B1-104-07` | File too large |
| `B1-105-10` … `B1-105-15` | File format not recognised — **six distinct codes, one identical label.** A2: the distinction *"est opportune pour traiter ces cas en interne"* |

`B1-104-02` … `-05` concern archives and therefore only arise on the **portal** path — the
API refuses archives outright (P4 §4.7).

###### SIRET referential codes

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `B1-201-01` | `Siret XXXXXX inconnu` |
| `B1-208-01` | `Siret XXXXXX radié au JJ/MM/AAAA` |

###### Batch-level code — `C_RET_AR_LOT`

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `0` / `00` | Correct |
| `11` | Version code incorrect (`C_VERSION` ∉ {`120`, `TST`}) |
| `15` | Message length incorrect (record ≠ 990) |

###### Per-declaration codes — `C_RETOUR_AR`

The full list from A4, which is also the norm-control code set from A2 §"Les contrôles de
norme":

| Code | Meaning | Field |
|---|---|---|
| `00` | **Correct — DPAE registered** | — |
| `01` | SIRET syntactically invalid | `N_SIRET` |
| `02` | URSSAF code invalid — not in Annexe II.1 | `C_URSSAF` |
| `03` | APE/NAF code invalid | `C_NAF_NN` |
| `05` | Company name missing or forbidden characters | `L_RAISON_SOC_1` |
| `06` | Address missing | `L_ADR_EMP_1` |
| `07` | Postcode not 5 numeric characters | `C_POSTAL_EMP` |
| `08` | Town missing | `L_BUR_DIST_EMP` |
| `11` | Application origin code incorrect | `C_APPLICATION` |
| `12` | Entry-origin code invalid / missing | `C_ORIG_SAISIE` |
| `31` | Employee surname missing | `L_NOM_PATRO_SAL` |
| `32` | Employee first name missing | `L_PRENOMS_SAL` |
| `33` | Date of birth invalid or missing | `D_NAISSANCE_SAL` |
| `34` | Place of birth missing | `L_LIEU_NAISS_SAL` |
| `35` | Hire date and/or time invalid or missing | `D_EMBAUCHE`, `H_EMBAUCHE` |
| `38` | NIR invalid — implausible, non-existent department, bad key | `N_SECU_SOC` |
| `39` | Declaration date invalid | `D_CREATION` |
| `40` | Declaration time invalid | `H_CREATION` |
| `43` | Sex invalid — not `M` or `F`, or missing | `C_SEXE_SAL` |
| `44` | Contract type invalid — not `1`, `2` or `3` | `C_TYPE_CONTRAT` |
| `45` | NAF code invalid for a CTT contract (must be `7820Z`) | `C_NAF_NN` |
| `46` | CDD end date mandatory / invalid / before the hire date | `D_FIN_CDD` |
| `47` | Department of birth invalid or missing | `C_DEPT_NAISS_SAL` |
| `50` | Occupational health service code invalid or missing | `C_MT_DCL` |
| **`98`** | **DPAE already exists** — duplicate on surname + first name + DOB + hire date + hire time + SIRET | — |
| **`99`** | **Accepted with errors on optional fields.** A case reference **is** issued | — |

Note code `11` appears in both the batch list and the per-declaration list with **different
meanings** (message version vs. application origin). Key error handling on the *field the
code came from*, never on the number alone.

###### The BIS (code 20)

A2 gives its semantics fully, and they are not what the name suggests:

- Produced **only** where the identity data diverges from the **SNGI** (Système National de
  Gestion des Identifiants). *"S'il n'y a pas d'anomalie relevée, aucun BIS n'est produit."*
  A3 confirms: *"Le BIS est produit en cas de divergence avec le SNGI."*
- Available at **J+1 after the hire date**.
- Carries two kinds of content: employees declared but **not identified** by the SNGI, and,
  where known, **the SNGI's own identification elements**, which *"doivent être utilisés
  dorénavant dans les déclarations sociales"*.
- Where an employee is unidentified, the employer must direct them to their local **CPAM** to
  obtain a NIR.
- Support for the BIS goes to **Carsat, Cnav or CGSS** — not to URSSAF (P2 §2.7).

> A2 puts a warning in a box, and it should be carried verbatim into the operations runbook:
> *"ce document n'est pas un accusé de réception de DPAE et, en aucun cas, vous ne devez
> effectuer une nouvelle DPAE pour ces salariés pour corriger les DPAE déjà enregistrées."*
>
> **A BIS must never trigger a re-filing.** The DPAE is already registered. Re-filing earns a
> code `98` and achieves nothing. The BIS is an instruction to fix the **employee master**,
> and its corrections propagate to the DSN — see INTERLINKAGES §2.2.

###### The four return scenarios

A3 enumerates every combination that can occur after a deposit:

| # | Sequence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **ARE** | Flow rejected at pre-control. Nothing was filed. |
| 2 | **AEE + BAN** | Flow accepted, every declaration failed norm controls. |
| 3 | **AEE + (BAN and/or CCO) + DPAE** | Flow accepted; some or all declarations conform; the AR follows. |
| 4 | **AEE + (BAN and/or CCO) + DPAE + BIS** | As 3, plus an identity divergence. |

A3 footnotes the *and/or*: *"Dans un fichier Dpae, une déclaration peut être déclarée conforme
et une autre être non conforme."* **BAN and CCO coexist for the same flow.** A state machine
that treats them as mutually exclusive at flow level is wrong — they are exclusive per
*declaration*, not per *flow*.

##### 5.5 Returns that must reach payroll (and what to do with each)

| Return | Reaches | Action |
|---|---|---|
| **ARE** | Ops, immediately | The whole file failed. **Do not retry the same bytes** — it is deterministic. Fix and re-deposit. |
| **BAN** (11) | Ops + data quality | Per-declaration norm failures. Each is an unfiled hire. **Statutory deadline is still running.** Escalate on the same day. |
| **CCO** (11) | Pipeline state only | Conformity, not registration. Not a completion signal. |
| **AR** (41), `C_RETOUR_AR = 00` | **Employee record — persist `R_DOSSIER`** | The hire is declared. **This is the only completion signal.** R.1221-9 obliges giving the employee a copy — see P7 §7.6. |
| **AR** (41), `= 99` | Data quality queue | Registered. Optional-field defect. **Do not re-file.** |
| **AR** (41), `= 98` | Reconciliation | Already declared. Verify our record has the earlier `R_DOSSIER`; if not, retrieve it. **Not an error.** |
| **AR** (41), other code | Ops, urgent | Unfiled hire with a named field defect (§5.4). Correct and re-deposit. |
| **BIS** (20) | **Employee master, and onward to the DSN** | Identity divergence. Apply the SNGI values. **Never re-file the DPAE.** |

###### Reconciliation must be per declaration

A file of 200 hires can yield one AEE, a BAN covering 3 declarations, a CCO covering 197,
one AR covering the 197, and a BIS covering 2 of them. **Nothing in that set is keyed to the
file.** The reconciliation key is the duplicate key — surname, first name, date of birth,
hire date, hire time, SIRET — plus `R_DOSSIER` once known.

A build that marks a batch "complete" on receipt of the AR will silently drop the 3 that
failed norm controls. Those three are unfiled hires on a pre-hire statutory deadline.

##### 5.6 Return payload layouts

This is where the gap is, and it is honest to state it plainly: **the interface is fully
specified; two of the four return payloads are not.**

| Return | Layout | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **10, 11** | DSN harmonised bilans format | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` — `dsn_bilans_v01r06.xsd`, named as A1 reference [3]. Distributed through the net-entreprises documentation area, **behind declarant login**. Already recorded as gated in the NETENT-DSN set (P5 §5.8 there) — one artefact, two runbooks, one request. |
| **41** (AR) | Format 120 (A2 Annexe 2: *"Accusé de réception de la DPAE … format 120"*) | **Partially derivable.** It is the same 990-byte record with `R_DOSSIER`, `C_RETOUR_AR`, `C_RET_AR_LOT` and Catégorie VI populated (P4 §4.3, §4.9). Whether the API-channel AR is byte-identical to the CFT-era AR is **UNVERIFIED**. |
| **20** (BIS) | A2 Annexe 2: *"Doc v 1.3 pour le BIS"* | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` — named by version only, **no public URL found**, no structure published anywhere obtained. |

**None of this blocks structural design**, and the sequencing is favourable: 10/11 share the
DSN parser; 41 is derivable well enough to build against and confirm on the first real
deposit; only 20 is genuinely opaque — and the BIS is conditional, low-volume, and its
*action* is known (apply SNGI values, never re-file) even where its bytes are not.

Sequence the resolution accordingly: request the bilans XSD with the DSN request; derive the
AR from A4 and verify it on the first `RÉEL` deposit; ask `api-dpae@acoss.fr` for the BIS
v1.3 specification at enrolment. See `effort-estimates.md` §6.

##### 5.7 Retention — an obligation on us, not on URSSAF

| Holder | What | Window | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| SI DPAE | Return **tracking** data (consultation service) | **13 months** | A1 |
| SI DPAE | Returns **available for download** | **3 months**, then *"purgés et ne sont pas archivés"* | A1 |
| Receiving body | The declaration data itself | **6 months** | R.1221-16 |
| **Employer** | The acknowledgement | **Until the employer-registration declaration is completed**, and available on inspector demand | R.1221-8, R.1221-12 |

The statutory retention (R.1221-8, open-ended) exceeds the technical retention (3 months) by
an unbounded margin. There is no reading under which the authority's storage discharges our
obligation.

> **Download every return well inside 3 months and archive it on our side.** After purge, a
> `telecharger-retour` call returns `404` — indistinguishable from a bad identifier (P3
> §3.5). The evidence the labour inspector may demand under R.1221-12 will simply not exist.
> P7 §7.6 makes this a scheduled job with an alert, not a best-effort.

R.1221-9 adds a second, separate obligation: **the employee must receive a copy** of the
declaration or the acknowledgement — satisfied if the employment contract names the
receiving body. That is an HR-process requirement this integration enables, not one it
discharges. Flag it to the client during implementation.

##### 5.8 Inbound build checklist

| # | Item |
|---|---|
| 1 | Parse the synchronous deposit response as **AEE or ARE**; capture `IdFluxDPAE` from the AEE (§5.3) |
| 2 | Persist `idFlux` durably — it is the only proven handle on a flow's returns (P3 §3.1) |
| 3 | Poll `lister-retours-flux` honouring `Expires`; never hard-code the interval (P3 §3.9) |
| 4 | **Take download URLs from the `<url>` element**, never construct them (§5.2) |
| 5 | Reuse the DSN bilans parser for codes 10 and 11 (§5.1) |
| 6 | Build a format-120 AR parser for code 41; verify against the first real deposit (§5.6) |
| 7 | Treat **`R_DOSSIER` as the sole completion signal** (§5.3, §5.5) |
| 8 | Classify `98` as idempotent and `99` as a pass — neither is a retry (§5.5) |
| 9 | Reconcile **per declaration**, on the duplicate key — never per file (§5.5) |
| 10 | Keep watching a flow for the BIS until **hire date + 2 days**, not until the AR (§5.2) |
| 11 | **Never re-file on a BIS.** Route SNGI corrections to the employee master (§5.4) |
| 12 | Route BIS support to Carsat/Cnav/CGSS, not `api-dpae@acoss.fr` (P2 §2.7) |
| 13 | Download and archive every return inside 3 months (§5.7) |
| 14 | Log raw `statut` and `nature` values on first contact to record the DPAE vocabulary (§5.2) |


### URSSAF-DPAE / `P6-test-conformance-cutover.md`

#### P6 — Test, Conformance & Cutover
**FR · URSSAF-DPAE**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · 1 unverified item
Source basis: A3 (test mode, deposit limits), A1 (return availability by environment),
A2 (control layers), A4/A5 (format), Code du travail R.1221-4 / R.1221-5 / D.1221-18

---

##### 6.1 Environments — there is only one

**There is no test environment, no sandbox, and no EDITEUR analogue for the DPAE.**

This is a finding, not a gap. A3 answers *"Comment réaliser un dépôt de test ?"* directly:

> *"Vous pouvez réaliser des tests à partir d'un compte client en déposant une DPAE de test
> sur le service de production."*

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Environments | **Production only** — `mon.urssaf.fr`, `*.dpae-edi.urssaf.fr` |
| Test mechanism | A **payload flag**, not a separate host (P4 §4.10) |
| Test account | A **real client account**. There is no test identity. |
| Formal conformance/certification gate | **None found.** No charte, no accreditation, no certification pass is described in any obtained artefact. |

Compare the DSN channel, which has a dedicated EDITEUR environment with its own service
codes (97 / 94). The DPAE has neither. Any plan that budgets an integration-test phase
against a sandbox is planning for an environment that does not exist.

###### What testing on production means, concretely

| Consequence | Handling |
|---|---|
| Every test call is a **real authenticated call from a real client's identity** | Test only with a client who has consented in writing. Record the consent. |
| `C_VERSION = TST` prevents the declaration from registering | **Verify the flag on every record before send.** It is the only thing standing between a test and a real statutory filing. |
| A test can never yield an `R_DOSSIER` | A1's matrix: returns **20** and **41** are `RÉEL`-only. Test proves parse + norm controls; it cannot prove registration (§6.5). |
| Errors are visible to the client on their Urssaf dashboard | Warn them. Unexplained rejects on their own dashboard generate support tickets against us. |
| There is no way to test the **business** layer (duplicates, `R_DOSSIER`) without filing for real | The first real filing **is** the business-layer test. Design cutover accordingly (§6.6). |

> **The most dangerous single value in this integration is `C_VERSION`.** `TST` and `120`
> differ by three characters and produce, respectively, nothing and a legally binding
> pre-hire declaration. Make it a deployment-level constant bound to the environment, never
> a per-record or per-request field, and assert it in the pre-flight gate (§6.3).

##### 6.2 Access prerequisites

There is no test-access step, because there is no test environment. The gate is simply
production enrolment (P2 §2.8):

| # | Prerequisite | Blocking? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Operating model decided — concentrateur recommended | Yes |
| 2 | Urssaf-en-ligne abonné registered; `nom`/`prénom` recorded exactly | Yes |
| 3 | Concentrateur registration email sent to `api-dpae@acoss.fr` and **confirmed** | **Yes — no published SLA** |
| 4 | A consenting client account for test deposits | Yes |
| 5 | `C_URSSAF` reference table obtained (P4 §4.5) | Before go-live, not before test |
| 6 | `C_APPLICATION` value for the API channel confirmed (P4 §4.3) | **Yes — test T-01 exists to settle it** |

> **UNVERIFIED — whether a conformance gate exists at all.** No obtained artefact describes
> one, and A3's answer to the testing question describes none. This is recorded as *not
> established from public sources*, **not** as a claim that URSSAF imposes no gate. Ask in
> the enrolment email (P2 §2.8 item 5).

##### 6.3 Pre-flight gate (before any HTTP call)

Twelve assertions, all local, all cheap. They exist because **the DPAE has no correction
model** (P4 §4.8): a defect that reaches URSSAF is fixed by telephone, not by a retry. The
gate is where quality is enforced on this channel.

| # | Assertion | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every record is **exactly 989 characters + one `LF`** = 990 bytes | Batch code `15`; A2 pre-control |
| 2 | `N_LG_MESS` = `912`, and the header occupies positions 1–77 | P4 §4.3 arithmetic |
| 3 | Output encoding is **ISO-8859-1**; no character encodes to more than one byte | A multi-byte character shifts every downstream field (P4 §4.2) |
| 4 | Line terminator is `LF`, never `CRLF` | A4, A5 |
| 5 | `C_VERSION` ∈ {`120`, `TST`} **and matches the deployment environment** | §6.1 |
| 6 | **`D_EMBAUCHE` is within 8 days ahead and not in the past** | **R.1221-4.** The format permits ±1 year; the *law* permits 8 days ahead. The channel will not enforce this. |
| 7 | Employee name fields contain only `A-Z`, `'`, ` `, `.`, `&`, `-` — uppercased and transliterated | Codes `31`/`32` (P4 §4.6) |
| 8 | `C_TYPE_CONTRAT = 3` ⇒ `C_NAF_NN = 7820Z` | Code `45` |
| 9 | `C_TYPE_CONTRAT = 1` ⇒ `D_FIN_CDD` present and **≥ `D_EMBAUCHE`** | Code `46` |
| 10 | `C_TYPE_CONTRAT ≠ 3` ⇒ `C_MT_DCL` present and in the SPST reference (or `MT01`/`MT999`) | Code `50` |
| 11 | `N_SECU_SOC` is 13 or 15 characters; if 15, **the key verifies**; consistent with `C_SEXE_SAL` and `D_NAISSANCE_SAL` | Code `38` |
| 12 | The **duplicate key** (surname + first name + DOB + hire date + hire time + SIRET) is not already registered locally with an `R_DOSSIER` | Pre-empts code `98` (P4 §4.6) |

Plus three structural assertions that are pure programmer error if they fail:

| # | Assertion |
|---|---|
| 13 | Every `Réservé` region and all of Catégorie VI (948–989) is blank |
| 14 | The payload is **not** an archive and **not** gzip-typed as content (gzip is `Content-Encoding`, not the format) |
| 15 | `C_ORIG_SAISIE` = `E` and `L_IDENT_EMT` does not begin with `UP` |

Assertion 6 is the one most likely to be omitted and the one with the clearest legal
consequence. Assertion 12 is the one that most reduces support load.

##### 6.4 Connectivity and contract tests

Eight tests. All against production — there is nowhere else. Run them in order; each
establishes a fact the next depends on.

| # | Test | Expected | Establishes |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-01 | `POST /authentifier_dpae` with valid credentials and `service=98` | `200`, body is a base64 token | The endpoint path, the credential quadruplet, the service code |
| C-02 | Inspect the C-01 response for an `Expires` header | **Expected absent or far-future** | Whether A3's "token never expires" holds. If a near expiry appears, P2 §2.5 and P7 §7.7 must be revised. |
| C-03 | `POST /authentifier_dpae` with a wrong password, **once only** | `401` + `WWW-Authenticate` | The DPAE's **actual realm strings** (P3 §3.6). **Run once.** Repeats trigger the 403 lockout. |
| C-04 | Deposit with **no** `Authorization` header | `401`, realm indicating a missing token | Token enforcement |
| C-05 | *(Concentrateur only)* Deposit on port **443** | `403` | Confirms the 8443 rule is real and that 403 ≠ entitlement problem (P3 §3.2) |
| C-06 | Deposit an **uncompressed** body | `400` or `415` | Confirms the gzip obligation carries over from the DSN |
| C-07 | `GET /lister-retours-flux/1.0/{unknown}` | `404` | Confirms 404 semantics; log the body shape |
| C-08 | `GET /lister-retours-flux/1.0/{real idFlux}` twice in quick succession | `200`, then check `Expires` and `Accept-Ranges` | **Reads the polling contract off the wire** — the interval is a server parameter and must never be hard-coded (P3 §3.9) |

**Log every raw response header and body on C-01 through C-08.** These eight calls are the
only opportunity to record the DPAE's actual vocabulary — realm strings, `statut` values,
error-body shape — none of which is published. Capture them into the runbook.

> C-03 and C-05 are deliberately single-shot. C-03 consumes a failed-authentication attempt
> against a lockout counter; C-05 uses a real client identity. Neither should be automated
> into a regression suite.

##### 6.5 Functional test cases

Two groups. `TST` cases prove the record; `RÉEL` cases prove the filing — and only `RÉEL`
cases can, because returns 20 and 41 do not exist in test (A1's matrix, P5 §5.1).

###### Group T — `C_VERSION = TST`, no registration occurs

| # | Case | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| T-01 | Minimal valid CDI, all mandatory fields | **AEE (10)** then **CCO (11)**. *Also settles `C_APPLICATION`* (P4 §4.3) — if the flow rejects at pre-control with code `11`, the value is wrong. |
| T-02 | Record of 989 characters (missing `LF`) | Pre-control failure, batch code `15` |
| T-03 | `C_VERSION = 110` | Rejected — versions 100/110 no longer accepted (A10) |
| T-04 | Valid CDD with `D_FIN_CDD` **before** `D_EMBAUCHE` | BAN (11), code `46` |
| T-05 | CTT with `C_NAF_NN ≠ 7820Z` | BAN (11), code `45` |
| T-06 | CTT with `C_MT_DCL` **absent** | **Accepted** — `C_MT_DCL` is not required for CTT |
| T-07 | Non-CTT with `C_MT_DCL` absent | BAN (11), code `50` |
| T-08 | `C_MT_DCL = MT999` (in-house service) | Accepted |
| T-09 | Employee surname containing an accent (`MUÑOZ`) | BAN (11), code `31` — **proves the transliteration requirement empirically** |
| T-10 | 15-character NIR with a deliberately wrong key | BAN (11), code `38` |
| T-11 | `C_POSTAL_EMP = 00000` | BAN (11), code `07` |
| T-12 | Multi-record file: 3 valid + 1 invalid | **AEE + BAN + CCO together** — proves partial acceptance and that BAN/CCO coexist (P5 §5.4) |
| T-13 | File emitted as UTF-8 with an accented employer name | Field shift → pre-control or norm failure. **Proves assertion 3.** |
| T-14 | A `.zip` containing a valid file | Rejected — the API refuses archives (P4 §4.7) |
| T-15 | Deposit, then `lister-retours-flux` on the returned `idFlux` | `200` with `<retour>` entries carrying `<url>` — **proves the hypermedia contract** |

T-12 and T-15 are the two that most directly validate the *inbound* design and should not be
skipped.

###### Group R — `C_VERSION = 120`, real filings, one consenting client

These cannot be avoided: `R_DOSSIER`, the duplicate rule and the BIS exist only in `RÉEL`.

| # | Case | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| R-01 | One genuine hire, correct in every respect | AEE → CCO → **AR (41) with `C_RETOUR_AR = 00` and a populated `R_DOSSIER`**. **This is the completion-signal test** (P5 §5.3). |
| R-02 | Re-deposit R-01's record verbatim | **AR with `C_RETOUR_AR = 98`.** Proves the duplicate key and that `98` must be classified as idempotent, not as an error (P5 §5.5). |
| R-03 | Valid hire with a defective **optional** field (e.g. over-long `N_TEL_EMP`) | **AR with `= 99` and an `R_DOSSIER` present.** Proves `99` is a pass. |
| R-04 | Hire of an employee with **no NIR** | AR registered; `I_IMMA` flag set in Catégorie VI. Proves the formality read-back (P4 §4.3). |
| R-05 | Hire with a plausible but SNGI-divergent identity | **BIS (20) at J+1 of the hire date.** Proves the BIS timing and the never-re-file rule (P5 §5.4). |
| R-06 | Retrieve every return for R-01 and archive it | Proves the archival path end to end (P7 §7.6) |

R-05 cannot be manufactured on demand — an SNGI divergence is a property of real identity
data. Treat it as an **observation case** during the pilot rather than a gating test, and
confirm the handling path in code review instead.

##### 6.6 Cutover

The DPAE is per-hire and pre-hire. There is no monthly boundary to cut over on and no
back-filling: **a missed DPAE cannot be filed late** — the obligation is discharged before
the employee starts work or not at all.

| Step | Action | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Enrolment confirmed (§6.2) | Blocking |
| 2 | Group T complete and green | Blocking |
| 3 | `C_APPLICATION` and `C_URSSAF` table settled | Blocking |
| 4 | R-01, R-02, R-03 complete on the pilot client | Blocking |
| 5 | **Parallel run**, pilot client: file via the API *and* keep the portal path live as the fallback. Reconcile every hire on `R_DOSSIER` before the employee's start date. | ≥ 2 weeks or 20 hires, whichever is later |
| 6 | Reconciliation: **100 % of pilot hires carry an `R_DOSSIER`** | Blocking — no tolerance |
| 7 | Ramp: pilot → 10 % of clients → all | One week per stage |
| 8 | Portal fallback stays documented and exercised **permanently** | Not retired |

> **Do not run a parallel period by dual-filing to the authority.** A DPAE filed twice
> returns `98` on the second — harmless, but it makes reconciliation ambiguous and it is not
> what "parallel" should mean here. The parallel run is *API filing with a portal fallback
> held ready*, not two filings.

###### The fallback is lawful and must stay

Three fallback layers exist, and each is a legitimate discharge of the obligation:

| Layer | Basis |
|---|---|
| Manual entry / file upload on **Urssaf en ligne** or **NetDPAE** | A2 — the portal path |
| **Cerfa 14738*01** by registered post, at the latest the last working day before the hire | **R.1221-5** |
| — | *(There is no third electronic channel: email ended 1 July 2022, CFT ended end-2022)* |

Under **D.1221-18**, a client filing more than 50 DPAE a year **must** file electronically —
so the paper fallback is an emergency measure, not an operating mode, and repeated use
exposes the client to the **D.1221-19** penalty (0.5 % of the monthly SS ceiling per
employee). Document the portal path as the real fallback and paper as the last resort.

##### 6.7 Post-cutover watch

| Window | Watch | Trigger |
|---|---|---|
| First 24 h after enrolment | `403` on deposit | **Expected** — registration propagates at J+1 (P3 §3.5). Do not alert. |
| First 2 weeks | `R_DOSSIER` coverage | **Any hire without one within 24 h of deposit** — page immediately |
| First 2 weeks | Raw realm strings, `statut` and `nature` values | Record the DPAE vocabulary into P3 §3.6 and P5 §5.2 |
| First month | Code `98` rate | A rising rate means the local duplicate check (assertion 12) is not working |
| First month | Code `31`/`32` rate | Means the transliteration rule is incomplete — a data problem, not a channel problem |
| First month | BIS arrivals | Confirms the poller is still watching flows past the AR (P5 §5.2) |
| First 3 months | Archive completeness | Every return downloaded and stored before purge (P5 §5.7) |
| Ongoing | Any `Expires` header on authentication | Would falsify the never-expires assumption (P2 §2.5) |


### URSSAF-DPAE / `P7-run-operate.md`

#### P7 — Run & Operate
**FR · URSSAF-DPAE**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · SLA unverified (not published)
Source basis: A1 (retention), A2 (BIS timing, controls), A3 (frequency, token, limits),
A4 (return codes), Code du travail R.1221-4 / R.1221-7 / R.1221-8 / R.1221-9 / R.1221-12 /
D.1221-18 / D.1221-19

---

##### 7.1 Operating calendar

There isn't one. **This channel is event-driven, not periodic** — and that is its defining
operational property.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Trigger | A hire is confirmed in the HR system |
| Deadline | **Before the employee begins work** (L.1221-10); at the earliest **8 days** before the foreseeable hire date (R.1221-4) |
| Catch-up | **None.** A DPAE cannot be filed late. The obligation is discharged pre-hire or breached. |
| Deposit frequency | Unconstrained. A3: *"Le mode API permet une utilisation au fil de l'eau."* |
| Monthly/annual cycle | None |
| Authority acknowledgement deadline | **5 working days** (R.1221-7); the acknowledgement becomes proof if not contested within **2 working days** |

The one recurring date that matters is not a filing date: under **D.1221-18**, a client's
electronic-filing obligation is determined by whether they filed **more than 50** DPAE in the
**preceding calendar year**. That is a January review item per client, not a payroll event.

> **The absence of a monthly cycle is a risk, not a relief.** A monthly filing announces its
> own failure — the deadline passes and someone notices. A per-hire filing that silently
> stops produces no calendar signal at all. The only thing standing between "the integration
> broke on Tuesday" and "we have 40 undeclared employees" is the `R_DOSSIER` reconciliation
> alarm in §7.4. Build it first.

##### 7.2 Job schedule

Four jobs. The window on each is set by the 8-day legal lead time, the 3-month purge, and the
J+1 BIS.

| Job | Cadence | Action | Why this cadence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **J1 — Deposit** | Continuous / near-real-time; at minimum **hourly** | Emit format-120 flow for hires confirmed since the last run; `POST /deposer-dsn/1.0/`; persist `idFlux` | The deadline is "before work starts". A daily batch cannot serve a same-day hire. |
| **J2 — Poll returns** | Every **`Expires`** interval, per open flow | `GET /lister-retours-flux/1.0/{idFlux}`; download anything new | The interval is a server parameter read off the header — never hard-coded (P3 §3.9) |
| **J3 — BIS sweep** | Daily | Re-poll flows whose declarations have `hire date ≤ today` and no BIS yet | The BIS appears at **J+1 of the hire date**, not of the deposit (A2). A flow deposited 8 days early stays open for 9 days. |
| **J4 — Archive** | Daily | Download and store every return not yet archived; verify none is older than **60 days** unarchived | 3-month purge with a 30-day safety margin (P5 §5.7) |

###### Flow lifecycle

A flow is **closed** only when every declaration in it has either an `R_DOSSIER` or a
terminal failure code, **and** the BIS window (hire date + 2 days) has passed for all of them.

| State | Entered when | Left when |
|---|---|---|
| `DEPOSITED` | AEE received, `idFlux` captured | CCO/BAN received |
| `CONFORMITY` | CCO and/or BAN received | AR received |
| `REGISTERED` | AR received; per-declaration outcomes recorded | BIS window elapsed |
| `CLOSED` | BIS window elapsed and all returns archived | — |
| `REJECTED` | ARE received | Corrected and re-deposited as a **new** flow |

Do not close on the AR. J3 exists precisely because the AR is not the last return.

##### 7.3 Error handling

The channel's errors split cleanly into three classes, and **only one of them is retryable**.

| Class | Examples | Policy |
|---|---|---|
| **Transient — retry** | `500`, `503`, connection reset, timeout | Exponential backoff, 3 attempts, then alert. Deposit and download have no rate restriction (P3 §3.9). |
| **Deterministic — never retry** | `422` (ARE), `415`, `400`, BAN codes, any `C_RETOUR_AR` ≠ `00`/`98`/`99` | **The same bytes will fail identically forever.** Route to a correction queue. Re-deposit only after the payload changes. |
| **Human action — never retry** | `401` with a password/account/entitlement realm, `403` entitlement | See §7.4. Automated retry here causes the **403 lockout**. |

###### The retry rules that matter

| Situation | Rule |
|---|---|
| `401`, realm contains `Jeton` or `Expiration du jeton` | Re-authenticate **once**, replay **once**. Then alert. |
| `401`, realm contains `Expiration du mot de passe`, `non-inscrit`, or names the portal | **Stop. Alert. Never retry.** Repeated attempts trigger the 403 account lockout (P3 §3.6). |
| `403` on deposit, **within 24 h of enrolment** | Expected — registration propagates at J+1. Wait, do not alert. |
| `403` on deposit, concentrateur | **Check the port before anything else.** Wrong port ≠ 8443 presents as 403 (P3 §3.2). |
| `429` on the list service | The `Expires` header was ignored. Fix the poller; do not increase concurrency. |
| `404` on download | The return is unknown **or purged**. If our archive has it, this is benign; if not, the evidence is gone (P5 §5.7). |
| `C_RETOUR_AR = 98` | **Not an error.** Reconcile to the earlier `R_DOSSIER`. Never re-deposit. |
| `C_RETOUR_AR = 99` | **Not an error.** Registered. Raise a data-quality item; never re-deposit. |
| Any BIS | **Never re-file.** Apply SNGI values to the employee master (P5 §5.4). |

> Three of the seven 401 outcomes and both of the "success with caveat" codes are things a
> naive handler gets wrong in the same direction: it retries. On this channel a retry either
> locks the account or earns a `98`. **The default must be "do not retry"**, with retry as
> the explicitly enumerated exception.

##### 7.4 Alerting

###### Page immediately

| Alert | Condition | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Undeclared hire** | Any hire with `D_EMBAUCHE ≤ today + 1` and no `R_DOSSIER` | A statutory pre-hire obligation about to be breached. **This is the alert that justifies the integration.** |
| **Deposit failing** | J1 fails 3 consecutive runs | Silent stoppage has no calendar signal (§7.1) |
| **ARE received** | Any avis de rejet | An entire file unfiled; deadlines still running |
| **Authentication human-action 401** | Password expired / account unknown / not registered | Blocks everything and cannot self-heal |
| **403 lockout** | `403` on authentication | The account is locked; every client on it is stalled |

###### Alert next business day

| Alert | Condition |
|---|---|
| BAN with any per-declaration failure | Each is an unfiled hire, but not yet at the deadline |
| Code `31`/`32` rate rising | Transliteration rule incomplete (P4 §4.6) |
| Code `98` rate rising | Local duplicate check failing (P6 §6.3 assertion 12) |
| Code `02` appearing | The `C_URSSAF` table has gone stale — mergers happen (P4 §4.5) |
| Code `50` appearing | The SPST reference is stale; refresh from open data (A6) |
| Unarchived return older than 60 days | Purge risk (§7.6) |

###### Do NOT alert

| Non-event | Why |
|---|---|
| `403` on deposit in the first 24 h after enrolment | Expected propagation delay |
| `C_RETOUR_AR = 99` | A pass with an optional-field defect |
| `C_RETOUR_AR = 98` | Idempotent duplicate |
| A CCO without an AR yet | Normal intermediate state |
| A flow with no BIS | The **normal** case — a BIS is produced only on SNGI divergence (A2) |
| Empty `<retours>` from the list service | Normal; returns are not instantaneous |
| `404` on a download whose return we already archived | Post-purge, benign |

The "do not alert" list is as load-bearing as the alert list. Four of the six non-events look
like failures at first glance, and a runbook that pages on them will be muted within a month
— taking the five real alerts with it.

##### 7.5 Service level

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Published availability | **None.** data.gouv.fr records availability as *"Non communiqué"*. A1 and A3 are silent. |
| Published rate limits | **None.** A3 states there is no recommended deposit frequency and no volume constraint beyond **100 MB per deposit**. |
| Polling limits | Yes, on the **list** service only, communicated dynamically via `Expires` and `Accept-Ranges` (P3 §3.9) |
| Maintenance windows | Not published |

**UNVERIFIED.** Do not assume the DSN's *"7j/7 24h/24, pas de plages de maintenance
récurrentes"* transfers — that is a GIP-MDS statement about GIP-MDS infrastructure, and these
are URSSAF hosts.

Design defensively: retry transient failures, hold undeposited hires in a durable queue, and
**never let an authority outage translate into a missed statutory deadline** — escalate to
the portal fallback (P6 §6.6) when J1 has been failing for more than 4 hours and a hire is
due within 24 hours.

##### 7.6 Archival — a Mercans obligation

The most under-appreciated requirement on this channel. Three independent instruments make
the *employer* responsible for evidence the *authority* will not keep:

| Instrument | Obligation |
|---|---|
| **R.1221-7** | The acknowledgement is the **proof of the declaration**; it becomes definitive if not contested within 2 working days of receipt |
| **R.1221-8** | The employer **retains** the acknowledgement until the employer-registration declaration is completed |
| **R.1221-12** | On a labour inspector's request, the employer **must produce** the acknowledgement or evidence that the declaration was made |
| **R.1221-9** | The employer must **give the employee a copy** of the declaration or the acknowledgement (satisfied if the contract names the receiving body) |

Against which the technical retention is:

| Holder | Window |
|---|---|
| SI DPAE — returns downloadable | **3 months**, then purged and *not archived* (A1) |
| SI DPAE — tracking data | 13 months (A1) |
| Receiving body — declaration data | 6 months (R.1221-16) |

> **After 3 months the proof does not exist anywhere except our archive.** A `404` from
> `telecharger-retour` is indistinguishable from a bad identifier (P3 §3.5) — the system
> cannot even tell you that it once held the document.

###### What must be archived, per declaration

| Item | Why |
|---|---|
| The **deposited record** (990 bytes, as sent) | Evidence of content and timing |
| `idFlux` and deposit timestamp | The only handle on the flow |
| The **AEE** (or ARE) | Evidence of receipt |
| The **CCO** or **BAN** | Evidence of conformity |
| The **AR (code 41)** with `R_DOSSIER` | **The statutory proof under R.1221-7** |
| The **BIS**, where produced | Evidence of the identity divergence and its resolution |
| The Catégorie VI formality flags | Evidence of which downstream bodies were engaged |

Retention on our side: **at least as long as the employment relationship plus the general
limitation period**, and in no case less than the client's own payroll-record retention
policy. Set it once, per client, in the implementation contract — do not let it default to
the 3-month technical window.

##### 7.7 Change management

| Change source | Signal | Action |
|---|---|---|
| **DPAE moves into the DSN** | Opening **postponed to January 2027**; régime général only, CDI and CDD only, CTT excluded; classic channels (EDI, API, EFI) **maintained** | **The standing watch item.** See INTERLINKAGES §2.3. Not a format change — a channel migration. |
| Format 120 revision | A new *dessin d'enregistrement* version | Versions 100 and 110 were **rejected**, not deprecated, when 120 arrived (A10). Assume the same discipline. |
| `C_URSSAF` table | URSSAF mergers — A4's history records several (`761+762+763=760`, `592+593+594=590`, `340+490`, …) | Refresh the table; a stale copy yields code `02`. Review annually. |
| SPST reference | New or closed occupational health services | Refresh from open data `sst_dpae` (A6); code `50` is the symptom |
| Token lifetime | A3 says the token never expires | If an `Expires` header or an `Expiration du jeton` realm ever appears, **P2 §2.5 is falsified** — treat as a design change, not an incident |
| TLS / CA change | Handshake failures | Unpublished for these hosts (P3 §3.8). Any TLS failure is a change-management event, not a transient. |
| Client crosses **50 DPAE/year** | January review | Electronic filing becomes **mandatory** (D.1221-18); paper fallback now carries the D.1221-19 penalty |

##### 7.8 Support and escalation

| Level | Owner | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | Mercans payroll ops | Pre-flight failures, transliteration defects, duplicate suppression, code `99` data quality |
| L2 | Mercans integration engineering | Transport errors, token handling, poller behaviour, parser defects |
| L3a | **`api-dpae@acoss.fr`** | API access, concentrateur registration, endpoint and format questions |
| L3b | The client's own **Urssaf**, via Urssaf en ligne | Account, subscriber identity, password, dashboard |
| L3c | **Carsat / Cnav / CGSS** | **BIS content and unidentified employees** (A2) |
| L3d | The employee's local **CPAM** | Obtaining a NIR for an employee the SNGI cannot identify (A2) |

Note that **three of the four L3 routes are not URSSAF**. A support process that funnels
everything to `api-dpae@acoss.fr` will stall on exactly the cases that need the most urgency
— identity anomalies affecting a new hire's social-security registration.

##### 7.9 Runbook — per-hire cycle

| Step | Action | Check |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Hire confirmed in HR | `D_EMBAUCHE` within 8 days ahead (R.1221-4) |
| 2 | Build the record | 15 pre-flight assertions pass (P6 §6.3) |
| 3 | Local duplicate check | Not already registered with an `R_DOSSIER` |
| 4 | Deposit | AEE received; `idFlux` persisted |
| 5 | Poll | CCO or BAN received; per-declaration outcomes recorded |
| 6 | Poll | AR received; **`R_DOSSIER` persisted against the employee** |
| 7 | **Gate** | **No `R_DOSSIER` by hire date − 1 → page** |
| 8 | Watch to hire date + 2 | BIS received? If yes → employee master, **never re-file** |
| 9 | Archive | Record, AEE, CCO/BAN, AR, BIS stored on our side |
| 10 | Hand off | Employee given a copy of the declaration or the AR (R.1221-9) |
| 11 | Close flow | All declarations terminal, BIS window elapsed, all returns archived |

##### 7.10 Monthly and annual review
