| Cadence | Item |
|---|---|
| Monthly | `R_DOSSIER` coverage = 100 % of hires; unarchived returns older than 60 days = 0; error-code distribution vs. previous month |
| Monthly | Any hire filed via the portal fallback — why, and is the pattern recurring? |
| Quarterly | Refresh the SPST reference (A6) and the `C_URSSAF` table |
| Annually (January) | Per client: did they file **> 50** DPAE last year? If so, D.1221-18 now binds and the paper fallback carries a penalty |
| Annually | Re-read A1/A2/A3 for revision — A1 has moved 2015 → 2017 → 2021, A2 is dated Sept 2025, A3 Oct 2023 |
| **Standing** | DSN *signalement DPAE* status vs. the January 2027 target (§7.7, INTERLINKAGES §2.3) |


### URSSAF-DPAE / `INTERLINKAGES.md`

#### INTERLINKAGES
**FR · URSSAF-DPAE ↔ NETENT-DSN, DGFIP-EDI, and the FR dossier artefacts**

This file is the **only** place in the URSSAF-DPAE runbook set where other authorities are
discussed. P1–P7 are scoped to `URSSAF-DPAE` alone. Nothing here is a build instruction for
another authority — each has (or needs) its own runbook.

---

##### 1. Authority map

| Authority | Channel | Filings | Runbook |
|-----------|---------|---------|---------|
| `NETENT-DSN` | REST API, net-entreprises.fr | 10 | Separate — `../NETENT-DSN/` |
| **`URSSAF-DPAE`** | REST API, `depot.dpae-edi.urssaf.fr` | 1 — `FR-URSSAF-001` | **This set** |
| `DGFIP-EDI` | EDIFACT via accredited partner | 1 — `FR-DGFIP-003` | Separate |

Three channels, three enrolments, three failure modes. The DPAE is the smallest of the three
by payload and the **strictest by deadline**: it is the only one whose obligation falls due
*before* an employee is paid at all.

---

##### 2. URSSAF-DPAE ↔ NETENT-DSN

These two channels are operated by different bodies, but they are deliberately convergent.
Understanding exactly *how far* the convergence goes is the difference between reusing half
the DSN build and building the wrong thing twice.

###### 2.1 What genuinely converges

URSSAF states the intent plainly (A2): the DPAE EDI offer *"évolue pour converger vers les
fonctionnalités proposées par le dispositif de la DSN"*, and A1 goes further — the API is
*"entièrement compatible avec le mode API disponible pour la DSN"*, with differences only in
addressing and exchanged elements.

| Element | Reusable from the DSN build? |
|---|---|
| HTTP interface shape — POST deposit, GET list, GET download | **Yes.** Same four service families, same resource names |
| `Authorization: DSNLogin` scheme, token in header | **Yes** |
| Concentrateur model — one token, per-call declarant identity, port 8443 | **Yes** |
| gzip obligation, chunked handling, `Expires`/`Accept-Ranges` polling | **Yes** ([DSN→] throughout P3) |
| HTTP status semantics and `WWW-Authenticate` realms | **Yes**, with DPAE wording unconfirmed (P3 §3.6) |
| Control typology — pre-controls, norm controls, business controls | **Yes** conceptually (A2 says so explicitly) |
| Return **10** and **11** payload formats | **Yes** — A3: *"identiques à ceux délivrés en DSN"*. Same bilans parser. |
| Retention rule — 13 months tracking / 3 months download | **Yes**, identical wording in A1 and the DSN guide |
| Reference client `ArchitectureDSN/dsn-api` | **As a structural reference only** (P3 §3.11) |

A team that has built NETENT-DSN has already built roughly the transport half of this.

###### 2.2 What does NOT converge — and where that bites

| Element | DSN | DPAE | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Enrolment** | Concentrateur charte with GIP-MDS | **Separate email registration** to `api-dpae@acoss.fr` — A3: *"même s'il est déjà inscrit à l'API DSN"* | **Two approvals, sequential.** The DSN charte grants nothing here. Most likely planning error on this integration. |
| **Authentication host and path** | `net-entreprises.fr` `/authentifier/1.0/` | `mon.urssaf.fr` `/authentifier_dpae` — **unversioned, different idiom** | Do not construct by pattern (P3 §3.2) |
| **Token lifetime** | Session-limited, `Expires` | **Never expires** (A3) | Different credential-storage posture (P2 §2.5) |
| **Deposit granularity** | **Unitary only** — one `S20` block per call | **Bulk, up to 100 MB** — designed for volume | **Opposite rules.** Carrying DSN habits across multiplies traffic; carrying DPAE habits back breaks the DSN (P4 §4.7). |
| **Archives** | Rejected | Rejected on the API; accepted on the *portal* path | A2's archive rules are portal-only — easy misread (P1 §1.6) |
| **Test environment** | Dedicated EDITEUR host, service codes 97/94 | **None.** Test flag on production (P6 §6.1) | Do not budget for a sandbox |
| **Payload** | NEODeS structured text, ISO-8859-1 | Format 120 fixed-width 990 bytes, or `FR_DUE_Upload` XML | Entirely separate emitter |
| **Correction model** | Annule-et-remplace, regularisation blocks | **None.** No cancel-and-replace; duplicates rejected with `98` (P4 §4.8) | A DPAE defect is a phone call, not a next-month fix |
| **Returns 20 / 41** | n/a | DPAE-proprietary formats (P5 §5.6) | Separate parsers, and two of them are `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` |
| **Cycle** | Monthly, calendar-driven | **Per hire, event-driven, pre-hire** | A stopped DSN announces itself; a stopped DPAE does not (P7 §7.1) |

###### 2.3 The boundary, and why it is easy to get wrong

The NEODeS norm **defines a DPAE signal** — `S20.G00.05` carries a *DSN Signal Déclaration
Préalable à l'Embauche* nature. It is in the 2026 specification. It is tempting to use.

> **Do not.** In 2026 the DPAE remains a **separate filing to URSSAF on its own channel.**

The net-entreprises knowledge base states the position directly: from 2027 it will be
possible to discharge the hiring-declaration obligation through a DSN signalement, but *"La
date de l'ouverture de ce service a été reportée à Janvier 2027"*, and *"Les canaux et les
modalités de transmission classiques sont maintenus : EDI, API et EFI."* Even then the scope
will initially be **régime général only**, and **CDI and CDD only — CTT excluded**.

`scope_manifest.json` records the same fact independently: the DPAE *"remains a SEPARATE
filing"* whose move into the DSN *"is deferred to 1 January 2027 at the earliest."* The
NETENT-DSN runbook guards the same boundary from its side (its P4 §4.3 and P6 §6.3
assertion 7).

**This is the one place where the NEODeS specification and current legal reality diverge, and
where a build that follows the spec literally files into the wrong channel.**

###### 2.4 What connects them operationally

| Link | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Same event, two filings** | A hire triggers a DPAE (pre-hire, ≤ 8 days ahead) *and* subsequently appears in the monthly DSN with an `S21.G00.40` contract block. Both must fire. |
| **Ordering** | **DPAE first, always.** It is a *pre*-hire declaration; the DSN is retrospective. |
| **Shared identifiers** | SIRET and NIR are common to both. |
| **Shared identity defects, shared return** | An identity divergence surfaces on **both** channels as a **BIS, code 20**, from the same SNGI check. Fixing it in the employee master fixes both. Fixing it per channel fixes neither properly. |
| **Shared SPST reference** | The DPAE's `C_MT_DCL` and the DSN's SPST rubric both draw on URSSAF's `MTnnn` vocabulary (open data `sst_dpae`). **One reference table, two consumers** — load it once. |
| **Shared portal, different services** | net-entreprises offers **NetDPAE**, a manual DPAE path. Same portal, different channel — not the DSN API, and not the DPAE API either. |
| **Independent enrolment** | See §2.2 row 1. |

###### 2.5 Watch item

When the DPAE folds into the DSN (**January 2027 at the earliest**), `URSSAF-DPAE` collapses
into `NETENT-DSN` and `FR-URSSAF-001` becomes an eleventh filing there. The NETENT-DSN set
tracks the same item (its P7 §7.7(4)); this set tracks it at P7 §7.7.

Two cautions for whoever executes that migration:

1. **It will be partial.** CTT is excluded at opening, and the régime agricole is out. A
   residual DPAE channel will still be needed for those hires — this runbook does not become
   obsolete on the switch-over date.
2. **It is a channel migration, not a format change.** The payload changes completely
   (format 120 → NEODeS block), the enrolment changes, the deadline does not.

---

##### 3. URSSAF-DPAE ↔ DGFIP-EDI

The two have almost nothing in common, and saying so precisely is the useful contribution.

| | **URSSAF-DPAE** | **DGFIP-EDI** |
|---|---|---|
| Filing | `FR-URSSAF-001` | `FR-DGFIP-003` |
| Subject | A **person** being hired | An **amount** of payroll tax |
| Trigger | Per hire | Monthly / quarterly / annual by amount |
| Conditional? | **No.** Every hire, every employer. | **Yes.** Only employers not liable to VAT on ≥ 90 % of turnover — otherwise **suppress, do not file nil** |
| Channel | REST API, URSSAF's own hosts | EDIFACT via a **DGFiP-accredited partner** |
| Access gate | Email registration, no accreditation | **Third-party accreditation** — a commercial dependency |
| Deadline character | **Pre-event and absolute** — cannot be filed late | Post-period; late filing is a penalty, not an impossibility |
| Data source | Employee master + contract | Payroll aggregate at employer level |
| Data flow between them | **None** | **None** |

###### Relative risk and sequencing

`URSSAF-DPAE` is small, cheap and **fully self-serviceable** — one email, no charte, no
accreditation. `DGFIP-EDI` is low-volume, conditional, and **gated behind third-party
accreditation** with unverified terms.

But the deadline character inverts the risk ordering:

> `DGFIP-EDI` can run manually indefinitely without breach. `URSSAF-DPAE` **cannot be caught
> up at all** — a DPAE not filed before the employee starts work is a breach that no later
> action repairs.

It also has a lawful portal fallback (P6 §6.6), so manual operation is not a breach — but
manual operation of a per-hire, same-day, pre-hire obligation across a client base is exactly
the kind of process that fails quietly. **That, not technical difficulty, is the argument for
building this integration.**

Recommended sequence across the three channels: **NETENT-DSN first** (it discharges ten of
twelve integrable filings), **URSSAF-DPAE second** (small, reuses the DSN transport, removes
a per-hire manual process), **DGFIP-EDI last** (conditional, low-volume, externally gated).

---

##### 4. Shared risks across all three

| Risk | NETENT-DSN | **URSSAF-DPAE** | DGFIP-EDI |
|------|-----------|-------------|-----------|
| Enrolment is a **human approval gate** with no published SLA | Yes (charte) | **Yes (email to `api-dpae@acoss.fr`)** | Yes (accreditation) |
| Test environment terms unverified | Yes | **N/A — there is no test environment at all** | Yes |
| Lawful **portal fallback** exists | Yes | **Yes** | Yes |
| Fallback is *sustainable* as an operating mode | Yes | **No — per-hire, same-day, and D.1221-18 makes it non-compliant above 50/year** | Yes |
| Identity data (SIRET, NIR) shared | Yes | **Yes** | SIRET only |
| Published SLA | Yes (7×24) | **No — "Non communiqué"** | Unverified |

**A defect in employee identity data propagates to all three.** Fix it once, upstream, in the
employee master — not per channel. On this channel the BIS (code 20) is the mechanism that
tells you about it, and it is the **same** SNGI check that produces the DSN's BIS.

---

##### 5. Links into the rest of the FR dossier

| Artefact | Relationship to this runbook set |
|----------|----------------------------------|
| `FR-compliance/scope_manifest.json` | Source of `FR-URSSAF-001`, its deadline and the DPAE-deferral fact. **P1 §1.7 records two refinements to it** — the deposit point is URSSAF's own host, not net-entreprises.fr, and the format is TXT **or** XML, not XML only. |
| `FR-compliance/03-statutory-integrations/integration-landscape.json` | The discovery output this set builds on. Its `known_constraints` (2027 deferral, closed email/CFT channels, portal fallback) are all confirmed and expanded here. |
| `FR-compliance/03-statutory-integrations/FR-SIR-INDEX-001-v1.0.docx` | One-row-per-authority index pointing here. |
| `FR-compliance/03-statutory-integrations/NETENT-DSN/` | The adjacent runbook set. **Its API guide v6.8 is a required reference for this one** (P3 §3.0). |
| `FR-compliance/04-reports-spec/` | Field-level specification. P4 is deliberately structural; the record layout is given because it *is* the structure, but business mapping belongs there. |
| `FR-compliance/05-data-dictionary/` | **Owns the transliteration rule.** P4 §4.6 establishes that employee name fields accept only `A-Z' .&-` — uppercased, unaccented, digit-free. That transformation must be specified once, in the DD, and reused by both channels. |
| `FR-compliance/01-ccg/` | Hiring-formality rules whose *execution* this channel performs. |
| `FR-compliance/integration-sources/urssaf-dpae/` | The downloaded artefacts and `_source-manifest.md`. |

###### Cross-artefact obligations this runbook creates

1. **Data Dictionary** must specify the employee-name transliteration to `A-Z' .&-`
   (uppercase, unaccented, no digits) as a shared rule — it is a DPAE hard requirement
   (codes 31/32) and good hygiene for the DSN.
2. **Data Dictionary / Report Spec** must model `C_MT_DCL` (SPST code) as a **first-class
   employee-contract attribute** sourced from URSSAF open data `sst_dpae`, not as a free-text
   field. It is mandatory for every non-CTT hire and it is validated (code 50).
3. **Data Dictionary** must carry **`R_DOSSIER`** as a persisted attribute of the hire event.
   It is the statutory proof of declaration (R.1221-7) and the reconciliation key (P5 §5.3).
4. **CCG** must document that the DPAE discharges **five** administrative acts at once
   (R.1221-2) — employer registration, employee registration, unemployment-insurance
   affiliation, SPST enrolment, and the pre-employment medical examination — and that
   omitting a NIR is the *mechanism* for requesting CPAM registration, not an error.
5. **CCG** must document the **D.1221-18 / D.1221-19** rule: more than 50 DPAE in the
   preceding calendar year makes electronic filing mandatory, with a penalty of 0.5 % of the
   monthly social security ceiling per employee.
6. **Report Spec** must not model a DPAE correction or cancellation transaction. **There is
   none** (P4 §4.8).
7. **Payslip / HR handover** must reflect **R.1221-9**: the employee receives a copy of the
   declaration or the acknowledgement, unless the contract names the receiving body.


### URSSAF-DPAE / `effort-estimates.md`

#### Effort Estimates
**FR · URSSAF-DPAE · URSSAF — DPAE deposit point**

Basis: the specification set actually in hand (source manifest A1–A12). Estimates are for a
**concentrateur** implementation (P2 §2.1) serving multiple clients, régime général only.

**Unit:** person-days of a developer already fluent in REST integration but **new to the
DPAE**. They exclude payroll-engine and HR-master work — sourcing correct *content* for the
record is Data-Dictionary scope, not integration scope (`INTERLINKAGES.md` §5).

**Two figures are given throughout:** *standalone*, and *after NETENT-DSN*. The second is
materially smaller, and the reason is structural rather than incidental — A1 delegates the
entire machine-mode interface to the DSN API guide, and A3 confirms that returns 10 and 11
use the DSN's own format. Roughly the transport half of this integration is the DSN's
transport half.

> **Confidence.** Build effort is estimated with reasonable confidence: the interface is
> fully specified and we hold the specification. **Two things are not confident** — the
> enrolment lead time (no published SLA) and the two unpublished return payload layouts
> (§5).

---

##### 1. Critical path is enrolment, not code

| Gate | Owner | Published SLA | Est. elapsed |
|------|-------|---------------|--------------|
| Urssaf-en-ligne abonné registration | URSSAF | None stated | Days |
| Concentrateur registration email → decision | **`api-dpae@acoss.fr`** | **None** | **Unknown — 1–4 weeks assumed** |
| Registration becomes effective | SI DPAE | **J+1**, inferred from the 403 *"attendre J+1"* rule | 1 day |
| Consenting pilot client identified | Mercans + client | n/a | Onboarding-dependent |
| `C_URSSAF` reference table obtained | URSSAF | None | Days, once asked |

> The **1–4 week** figure is an **assumption, not a sourced fact.** Nothing published states a
> turnaround. It is narrower than the DSN's assumed 2–8 weeks because the procedure is
> materially lighter — an email with four data items, not a signed charte reviewed by a
> committee — but it is still a human decision with no commitment attached.
>
> **Send the registration email on day 1**, in parallel with development, and put the two
> open questions in the same message (P2 §2.8 item 5): the TLS/CA requirement, and
> confirmation of the abonné-registration reading.

Development can proceed against the specification without access. **Only testing is blocked**
— and note that testing here means *production* testing (P6 §6.1), so it is blocked on
enrolment *and* on a consenting client, not on a sandbox.

---

##### 2. Build effort by phase

| Phase | Work | Standalone | After NETENT-DSN |
|-------|------|-----------:|-----------------:|
| **P1** | Understand channel, filing, legal deadlines and constraints (this runbook substitutes for most discovery) | 1 | 0.5 |
| **P2** | Credential store, encrypted-at-rest password, concentrateur `declarant=` header, **indefinite-token handling with a live 401 path**, service-code config | 3 | **1** |
| **P3** | HTTP client: 4 endpoints, port-8443 rule, gzip, chunked, `Expires`/`Accept-Ranges` polling governor, **`WWW-Authenticate` realm parse**, retry-class policy | 6 | **2** |
| **P4** | Format-120 writer: ISO-8859-1, fixed-width emitter, 24 fields + reserved regions, **transliteration**, SPST + `C_URSSAF` reference loading, **15-assertion pre-flight gate**, duplicate suppression, deposit call, 422 handling | **9** | 8 |
| **P5** | Returns: list-then-hypermedia-download, **bilans parser (reuse)**, **format-120 AR parser (new)**, **BIS handling (new)**, `R_DOSSIER` reconciliation per declaration, BIS-window flow lifecycle, archive writer | 9 | **6** |
| **P6** | Test harness: 8 connectivity + 15 `TST` + 6 `RÉEL` cases, pilot-client coordination, cutover rehearsal | 6 | 5 |
| **P7** | Event-driven scheduling, BIS sweep, alerting incl. the do-not-alert set, statutory archival, per-hire runbook, change watch | 5 | 4 |
| | **Build subtotal** | **39** | **26.5** |

###### Contingency

| Item | Days |
|------|-----:|
| `C_APPLICATION` resolution and any rework it forces (P4 §4.3) | 1 |
| AR (code 41) parser rework once the real payload is seen (P5 §5.6) | 2 |
| BIS (code 20) parser built from first observation rather than specification | 3 |
| Bilans XSD integration once obtained; possible rework | 2 |
| First-contact defects — spec-vs-behaviour divergence on a channel with no sandbox | 3 |
| **Contingency subtotal** | **11** |

###### Total

| | Standalone | After NETENT-DSN |
|---|-----------:|-----------------:|
| Build | 39 | 26.5 |
| Contingency | 11 | 11 |
| **Total engineering** | **50 person-days** | **37.5 person-days** |

Elapsed, one developer, enrolment in parallel: **8–12 weeks standalone**, **6–9 weeks after
NETENT-DSN** — in both cases dominated by the enrolment gate and the pilot-client parallel
run (P6 §6.6 step 5), **not** by the days of work.

Contingency does not shrink with DSN reuse, and that is deliberate: every contingency item
is DPAE-specific. The reuse is in the transport, not in the risk.

---

##### 3. Where the effort concentrates

**P4 is the largest phase (9 d) and does not shrink with DSN reuse.** That is the correct
shape and it is worth saying why, because "990 bytes, 24 fields" reads like a day's work:

- **Fixed-width emission is unforgiving.** 989 characters exactly, ISO-8859-1, `LF`, every
  reserved region blank, three arithmetic invariants that must tie out (P6 §6.3). One
  multi-byte character shifts every downstream field.
- **The transliteration rule is real work.** Employee name fields accept `A-Z' .&-` only —
  uppercase, unaccented, digit-free. Getting `MUÑOZ`, `Ø`, `ß`, hyphenated double-barrels and
  CJK names right is the difference between a working integration and a steady drip of code
  31/32 rejections.
- **Two reference tables must be loaded, not hard-coded** — SPST from open data (409 rows,
  changes) and `C_URSSAF` (mergers happen; A4's own history records four).
- **The pre-flight gate has 15 assertions** and they are not optional decoration. There is
  **no correction model** (P4 §4.8): a defect that reaches URSSAF is fixed by telephone. The
  gate is where quality is enforced on this channel.

**P5 is second (9 d / 6 d) and half of it is unbuildable from documentation.** Returns 10 and
11 reuse the DSN's bilans parser; return 41 must be inferred from A4's record layout and
confirmed against the first real deposit; return 20's layout is not published anywhere we
could reach. The 6-day figure assumes the bilans parser exists; it does not assume the AR or
BIS parsers can be written correctly first time — that is what the contingency covers.

**P2 and P3 collapse hardest with DSN reuse** (3+6 → 1+2). Same auth scheme, same header
conventions, same polling governor, same status semantics. Only the endpoints, the
`/authentifier_dpae` path shape and the never-expiring token differ.

---

##### 4. Cost of getting it wrong

Ranked by consequence, not by likelihood.

| Defect | Consequence |
|---|---|
| **Treating HTTP 200 as "filed"** | Undeclared employees. `R_DOSSIER` on the AR is the only proof (P5 §5.3). The failure is invisible until a labour inspector asks under R.1221-12 — by which time it is systematic. |
| **`C_VERSION = TST` in production, or `120` in test** | Either every hire silently unfiled, or a real statutory declaration made during a test. Three characters apart (P6 §6.1). |
| **Reconciling per file instead of per declaration** | A 200-hire file yielding 197 CCO + 3 BAN marks "complete". Three pre-hire obligations breached, silently (P5 §5.5). |
| **Re-filing on a BIS** | A2 warns against it in a box. Earns code `98`, achieves nothing, and masks the real action — fixing the employee master. |
| **Retrying a human-action 401** | Locks the concentrateur account. **Every client on that account stalls at once** (P3 §3.6, P7 §7.3). |
| **Not archiving inside 3 months** | The proof required by R.1221-7/R.1221-12 ceases to exist. The authority purges and does not archive (P5 §5.7). |
| **Emitting UTF-8** | Every field after the first accented character is shifted. Fails loudly — the cheap kind. |
| **Missing the 8-day window** | The format allows ±1 year; **R.1221-4 allows 8 days**. Passes every technical control and breaches the law (P4 §4.6). |
| **Treating `99` as a failure** | Noise, then a re-send, then a `98`. Registered hires flagged as broken. |
| **Assuming DSN enrolment carries over** | Weeks lost. A3 says it does not, in as many words. |
| **Building the XML variant without the XSD** | Different value domain from the flat variant (`CDI` vs `2`), no offline validation, one example to work from (P4 §4.1). |

Note the asymmetry with the DSN: there, the expensive failures are *quiet and monthly*
(wrong withholding rate). Here they are *quiet and per-person* — and each one is an
individual employee working without a valid pre-hire declaration.

---

##### 5. Open items and their resolution path

| Ref | Item | Type | Resolution | Blocks |
|-----|------|------|------------|--------|
| P4 §4.3 | `C_APPLICATION` value for the API channel | **UNVERIFIED** | Ask in the enrolment email; settle empirically with test **T-01** | Real filing — but a wrong value fails loudly at pre-control |
| P4 §4.5 | `C_URSSAF` table (Annexe II.1) | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` | Ask `api-dpae@acoss.fr`; or retry `fichierdirect.declaration.urssaf.fr` from an unblocked network | Go-live |
| P5 §5.6 | `dsn_bilans_v01r06.xsd` | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` | **Bundle with the NETENT-DSN schema-pack request** — one artefact, two runbooks | Parser validation only |
| P5 §5.6 | BIS (code 20) format, "Doc v1.3" | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` | Ask `api-dpae@acoss.fr` at enrolment; otherwise build from first observation | BIS parsing only — conditional, low volume |
| P5 §5.6 | AR (code 41) exact API-channel layout | **UNVERIFIED** | Derivable from A4; confirm on test **R-01** | Nothing — derivation is good enough to build |
| P4 §4.1 | `DUE_Upload.xsd` | `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` | Ask at enrolment | **Nothing** — we build the flat variant |
| P3 §3.8 | TLS version and CA chain for the DPAE hosts | **UNVERIFIED** | Ask at enrolment; confirm on test **C-01** | Nothing — assume TLS 1.2+ and public CAs |
| P3 §3.1 | Per-declarant returns-search endpoint | **UNVERIFIED** | Ask at enrolment | Nothing — build on the per-flow path |
| P3 §3.6 | DPAE `WWW-Authenticate` realm wording | **UNVERIFIED** | Capture on test **C-03** | Nothing — match on substrings |
| P2 §2.2 | Abonné-registration wording ambiguity in A3 | **UNVERIFIED** | Confirm in the enrolment email | Nothing |
| P6 §6.2 | Whether any conformance gate exists | **UNVERIFIED** | Ask at enrolment | Nothing — none is described |
| P7 §7.5 | SLA, availability window, rate limits | **UNVERIFIED — not published** | Ask at enrolment | Nothing — design defensively |

**Twelve open items, and only two block go-live.** Nine of the twelve can be closed by a
single well-composed enrolment email; two more close on the first test run. That ratio is the
practical reason this integration is worth doing rather than deferring.

---

##### 6. Assumptions

1. **Concentrateur model** (P2 §2.1). The declarant model would add per-client credential
   custody — materially more work and materially more risk, for no functional gain.
2. **Format 120 flat, not XML** (P4 §4.1). The XML variant would add the XSD as a hard
   blocker and a second value domain.
3. **Régime général only.** MSA/régime agricole is a different deposit point and out of
   dossier scope.
4. **A consenting pilot client is available.** Without one there is no way to test at all —
   there is no sandbox. This is a commercial prerequisite, not a technical one.
5. **The DSN bilans parser exists** in the "after NETENT-DSN" column. If NETENT-DSN is not
   built, use the standalone column.
6. Enrolment turnaround of **1–4 weeks**. Unsourced (§1).
7. Employee master data quality is adequate. **Not free:** the transliteration rule and NIR
   key validation will surface pre-existing defects, and the first month's code 31/32/38 rate
   is a data-quality signal, not an integration defect.

---

##### 7. Recommended sequence

| Week | Action |
|------|--------|
| 1 | Send the concentrateur registration email to `api-dpae@acoss.fr` **with all five open questions**. Register the Urssaf-en-ligne abonné. Identify the pilot client and obtain written consent. |
| 1–4 | Build P4 (writer, transliteration, reference tables, pre-flight gate) — **independent of enrolment** |
| 2–5 | Build P2/P3 (auth, transport) — reuse the DSN client where NETENT-DSN exists |
| 4–7 | Build P5 (returns, `R_DOSSIER` reconciliation, archival) — bilans parser reused, AR parser derived |
| On access | Run **C-01…C-08**; capture realm strings, `Expires`, `Accept-Ranges` |
| +1 week | Run **Group T** (15 `TST` cases). T-01 settles `C_APPLICATION`. |
| +1 week | Run **R-01…R-04** on the pilot client. R-01 proves `R_DOSSIER`; R-02 proves the duplicate rule. |
| +2 weeks | **Parallel run** on the pilot: API filing, portal fallback held ready. 100 % `R_DOSSIER` coverage required to proceed. |
| Then | Ramp pilot → 10 % → all, one week per stage. **Keep the portal fallback documented and exercised permanently.** |

Total elapsed from day 1 to full ramp: **8–12 weeks standalone, 6–9 after NETENT-DSN**, with
the enrolment reply the single largest unknown.

---

##### Deliverables

| Phase | File | Covers | Status |

| **P1** | [`P1-authority-channel-profile.md`](P1-authority-channel-profile.md) | The authority, the two forms and their rhythms, the conditional-liability constraint, channel classification, five load-bearing constraints, deposit granularity, and two corrections to the dossier's own records | BUILD-READY · 1 deadline discrepancy |
| **P2** | [`P2-enrolment-access-credentials.md`](P2-enrolment-access-credentials.md) | Model A vs Model B vs EFI, the employer's own prerequisites, the habilitation dossier, the **face-to-face accreditation**, the EDIFICAS attestation, standing obligations, support routing | BUILD-READY · 2 administrative unknowns |
| **P3** | [`P3-transport-interface-contract.md`](P3-transport-interface-contract.md) | Why there is no request/response interface, **two transport generations** (PassTrans / POSEIDON), directory contract, timing semantics, the securing layer, test-mode flags, retry semantics — every row tagged **[CURRENT]** / **[LEGACY]** / **[PROVISIONED]** | BUILD-READY · 3 provisioned |
| **P4** | [`P4-outbound-payload-infent-dp.md`](P4-outbound-payload-infent-dp.md) | `INFENT DP` structure, the **25-character `IND` codification**, `P-IDENTIF`, the ROF, **2501 (3 fields)**, **2502 (76 fields)**, the payment block, échéance rules, the substitution model, a **35-assertion pre-flight gate**, millésime management | BUILD-READY · **2026-current** |
| **P5** | [`P5-inbound-returns-contrl-infent-cr.md`](P5-inbound-returns-contrl-infent-cr.md) | The three return messages, the **four control levels and their blast radius**, the **seven code-bilan values**, the `AFL DEC` ≠ `AFL TP` trap, the recovery matrix, the error catalogue, evidentiary value and retention | BUILD-READY · 1 code-numbering gap |
| **P6** | [`P6-test-conformance-cutover.md`](P6-test-conformance-cutover.md) | Test as a flag on production (with **all four levels still running**), four gates, the **DGFiP fictitious test population** and its three TS cohorts, 31 test cases, cutover with a lawful fallback, post-cutover watch | BUILD-READY · 1 gap (test bank details) |
| **P7** | [`P7-run-operate.md`](P7-run-operate.md) | The **derived** schedule, 6 jobs, deposit lifecycle, retry classes, **10 failure modes ranked by how badly they mislead**, alerting **and the do-not-alert set**, dual-store archival, change management, per-échéance runbook | BUILD-READY · SLA unpublished |

##### Supporting

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| [`INTERLINKAGES.md`](INTERLINKAGES.md) | Cross-references to `NETENT-DSN` and `URSSAF-DPAE`, and links into the rest of the FR dossier. **Only** file discussing other authorities |
| [`effort-estimates.md`](effort-estimates.md) | **74 person-days** for Model A; **~17–23** via an accredited partner; **~2** for portal-only. 6–9 months elapsed, gated by accreditation — with a recommendation |
| [`../../integration-sources/dgfip-edi/_source-manifest.md`](../../integration-sources/dgfip-edi/_source-manifest.md) | Every downloaded artefact with SHA-256 and source URL, plus **three publication defects** found in the authority's own site |

---

##### Read this first

Five things about this integration are counter-intuitive and cause most failures:

1. **DGFiP will not accept a file from Mercans.** Only an accredited **partenaire EDI** may
   transmit, and accreditation ends with a **face-to-face appointment in France** where a USB key
   is handed over. This is the critical path, it is administrative, and no engineering shortens
   it. There are two lawful alternatives — contract an accredited partner, or file on the portal.
   → P2 §2.1, §2.4; `effort-estimates.md` §1, §4
2. **The payment can succeed while the filing fails.** Code bilan **`Rejet TD`** means the
   declaration was rejected and the télérèglement accepted: the money moves, nothing is declared,
   the bank statement agrees, and a single success flag reports success. Model the outcome as a
   **pair** — `(declaration, payment)` — never one boolean. → P5 §5.4, P7 §7.4
3. **Correction is destructive substitution, not amendment.** Before the DLS, a later deposit
   *replaces* the earlier one, which is then treated as never filed and **disappears from the
   client's portal view**. And a replacement that omits the payment data **cancels an accepted
   payment**. Blind retry is unsafe. → P4 §4.9, P7 §7.3
4. **For most clients, the correct behaviour is to file nothing.** The taxe sur les salaires is
   due only from employers not VAT-liable on ≥ 90 % of turnover, and there is a franchise floor
   below it. A non-liable client must be **suppressed, not filed nil** — a blank form is rejected
   outright. → P1 §1.3, P7 §7.1
5. **Test mode is a flag on production, and it is opt-in.** `UNB/0035=1` **and** `UNG/0040=DP3`,
   both mandatory. A file that omits them is a **real filing**. The upside: test deposits run all
   four control levels and return a real code bilan, so the whole chain is provable before
   go-live. → P3 §3.8, P6 §6.1

---

##### Source basis

| Artefact | Reference | Date |
|----------|-----------|------|
| EDI-Paiement **Volume III-C — Flux TS** | v1.3 – 2021-12 | upd. 10/01/2022 |
| EDI-Paiement Volume IV — technical transfer guide | v1.0 – 2021-12 | upd. 15/09/2021 |
| EDI-TVA & PAIEMENT Volume II Commun — organisational & legal | v1.0 – 2021 | June 2021 |
| EDI-Paiement Volume III — forms & codes (generic) | v1.0 – 2021 | March 2021 |
| **EDI-Paiement Volume III — millésime 2026** (EDIFICAS) | **v2025-12 – V1.0** | pub. 20/01/2026 |
| **`PAYE26D` / `PAYE26F` autotest structure tables** | **millésime 2026** | 05/2026 |
| Form specifications 2501 / 2502 | Version 2022 du 01-01-2022 V2 | 2022 |
| PassTrans technical prerequisites — SFTP and PESIT | **v1.1** | **04/06/2026** |
| POSEIDON FTPS parameter guide | v5.1 | undated |
| Pack technique nouvelle sécurisation EDI (worked example) | v1.1 | — |
| Tables population fictive PEDI | **v9.7** | — |
| Liste des partenaires EDI actifs — **349 partners** | — | — |

Full manifest with SHA-256 checksums, retrieval URLs and the publication defects found:
[`_source-manifest.md`](../../integration-sources/dgfip-edi/_source-manifest.md).

**Nothing was blocked.** No login, certificate or CAPTCHA was required or attempted, no live
service endpoint was contacted, and **no `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` marker is carried anywhere in this
runbook set.** The open items in `effort-estimates.md` §6 are questions of *staleness,
non-publication and provisioning* — not of access.


### DGFIP-EDI / `P1-authority-channel-profile.md`

#### P1 — Authority & Channel Profile
**FR · DGFIP-EDI · DGFiP — taxe sur les salaires (2501-SD / 2502-SD) via the EDI channel**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · Mode: structural · Directions: outbound + inbound
Source basis: EDI-Paiement Volume III-C *Flux TS* v1.3-2021-12, Volume IV v1.0-2021-12,
Volume II Commun 2021, Volume III 2021 + **Volume III 2025-12 v1.0 (millésime 2026)**,
the 2501/2502 form specifications, and the **PAYE26D/PAYE26F autotest structure tables**.
Scope: `DGFIP-EDI` only. NETENT-DSN and URSSAF-DPAE are cross-referenced in `INTERLINKAGES.md`.

---

##### 1.1 What this authority is

The **Direction Générale des Finances Publiques** is the receiving authority in its own right.
It is not a routing platform: it levies, receives, adjudicates and collects.

This entry covers **one tax only — the taxe sur les salaires**. DGFiP's other payroll-adjacent
obligation, withholding at source (*prélèvement à la source*), does not travel here: it rides
inside the monthly DSN and is `NETENT-DSN`'s business. Two DGFiP obligations, two entirely
different channels, no shared code. → `INTERLINKAGES.md` §2.

The structural fact that shapes everything below is that **DGFiP does not accept files from
taxpayers or their payroll providers.** It accepts files only from a party it has itself
accredited as a **partenaire EDI**. There is no public API, no client-certificate self-service,
no sandbox to sign up for. The channel is closed by design, and entry to it is an
administrative act performed in person in France (P2 §2.3).

##### 1.2 Filings carried by this channel

**One** in-scope filing from `scope_manifest.json` rides this integration — but it is carried by
**two different forms on two different rhythms**, which the manifest's single row conceals:

| Filing ID | Form | Direction | Frequency | Carried how |
|-----------|------|-----------|-----------|-------------|
| `FR-DGFIP-003` | **2501-SD** — *relevé de versement provisionnel* | outbound | monthly or quarterly, by amount | `INFENT DP`, document code `2501`, flux `TS` |
| `FR-DGFIP-003` | **2502-SD** — *déclaration annuelle de liquidation et régularisation* | outbound | annual, or within 60 days of cession/cessation | `INFENT DP`, document code `2502`, flux `TS` |
| *(same filing)* | — | **inbound** | per interchange, then ≤ 2 working days | `INFENT RCS`, `CONTRL`, `INFENT CR`, each wrapped in `AUTACK` |

Every deposit additionally carries exactly one **`P-IDENTIF`** form — the identification and
payment carrier. It is not optional and it is not repeatable (P4 §4.3).

###### Statutory rhythm and deadlines

| Prior-year tax | Instrument | Deadline |
|----------------|-----------|----------|
| **< €1,200** | *nothing* — franchise, no filing at all | — |
| **< €4,000** | annual **2502-SD** only | 15 January *(see §1.8 discrepancy)* |
| **€4,000 – €10,000** | **3 × 2501-SD** (quarterly) + closing 2502-SD | **15 April, 15 July, 15 October** |
| **> €10,000** | **11 × 2501-SD** (monthly, Feb–Dec) + closing 2502-SD | 15th of the following month |
| any | closing **2502-SD** | **31 January** of the following year |
| cession / cessation | **2502-SD** | **cession date + 60 days** |

> **January is never an acompte.** Volume III-C is explicit: the monthly échéances run
> *15 février … 15 décembre*, "le 12ème mois est liquidé par le solde", and "le mois de janvier
> n'est donc jamais le sujet d'un acompte". A scheduler that emits twelve 2501-SDs a year is
> wrong, and DGFiP will reject the January one on échéance grounds.

Electronic filing and electronic payment are **compulsory** — CGI art. **1649 quater B quater**
and **1681 septies**, with art. **369 of Annex III** for the taxe sur les salaires specifically.
Paying a compulsorily-teleregulated tax by any other means attracts penalties in its own right
(Volume II §2.1.4.1).

##### 1.3 The conditional-filing constraint

The taxe sur les salaires is due only from employers **not liable to VAT on at least 90 % of
turnover** — banks, insurers, associations, mutuals, health bodies, holding companies.

This makes `DGFIP-EDI` the only integration in the FR dossier whose correct behaviour for most
clients is **to file nothing at all**. Three consequences the design must carry:

1. **Liability is a per-client, per-year determination**, not a payroll computation. It must be
   an explicit configured fact with an owner and a review date, never inferred.
2. **A non-liable client must be SUPPRESSED, not filed nil.** There is no nil 2501-SD. The
   coherence control `B147` rejects an entirely blank form outright, and DGFiP's own error list
   carries *"Vous ne pouvez adresser un formulaire entièrement vide"*.
3. **The franchise is a second suppression gate.** Below €1,200 of annual tax there is no
   filing even for a liable employer.

A build that treats this integration as "run it for everyone, it'll come out zero" produces
rejected deposits for the majority of the client base. → P7 §7.1.

##### 1.4 Channel classification

| Property | Value | Source |
|----------|-------|--------|
| Channel type | **Batch file exchange via an accredited intermediary** — not an API | Vol II §2.4, Vol IV §4.1 |
| Message standard | **UN/EDIFACT** | Vol IV §4.1.1 |
| Outbound message | `INFENT DP` — a subset of `INFENT`, directory **D00B** | Vol IV §4.1.1.1 |
| Securing message | `AUTACK` — EDIFACT v4 part 6 | Vol IV §4.1.1.1, §4.3 |
| Inbound messages | `INFENT RCS`, `CONTRL` (v4 part 4), `INFENT CR` — each with its own `AUTACK` | Vol IV §4.1.1.1 |
| Transport | **PassTrans** (Axway SecureTransport) over **SFTP** or **PESIT**; legacy **POSEIDON** over **FTPS** or **CFT/IP** | A12, A13, A14, Vol II §2.5.1 |
| Protocol choice | **One protocol only, for both directions** — "le partenaire ne peut exploiter qu'un seul mode de transmission" | Vol II §2.5.2 |
| Encryption of payload | **None.** Files are *not* encrypted; confidentiality comes from the transport | Vol II §2.7.2.3 |
| Integrity / non-repudiation | **RSA-2048 signature over SHA-256** of the functional group ‖ timestamp | A15 `signer_donnees.c` |
| Authentication | DGFiP-issued **electronic accreditation**, delivered face-to-face | Vol II §2.4.3.2.1.2 |
| Availability | **Continuous — every day including weekends and public holidays** | Vol II §2.5.2 |
| Return SLA | Processing reports within **2 working days** of receipt at the ESI | Vol II §2.5.2, §2.7.2.1 |
| Separate test environment | **None.** Test is a flag on the production channel | Vol IV §4.5.4 |
| Max transfer size | **4 GB** per transfer (PassTrans) | A12 §3 |
| Archival by DGFiP | **6 years**, in the original signed format | Vol II §2.7.3.1 |

**SOAP/WSDL/XSD: none exist.** This is a flat-file EDIFACT channel. Any solution design that
assumes a web service for this authority is designing for the wrong channel.

##### 1.5 Constraints that shape the design

Five constraints are load-bearing and must reach the solution design, not just the runbook.

1. **Access is an accreditation, and it is granted in a room in France.**
   Becoming a partenaire EDI requires a convention conforming to a model fixed by *arrêté*, filed
   with the regional DRFiP correspondent for the region of the applicant's registered office, and
   then a **physical face-to-face appointment** at which the applicant's representative hands over
   a USB key and receives the electronic accreditation on it (Vol II §2.4.3.2.1.2). No remote path
   is published. This is the single largest unknown for a non-French provider and it is
   **administrative, not technical** — no amount of engineering shortens it. → P2 §2.3, §2.9.

2. **Transfer success does not exist here, and neither does a single acceptance signal.**
   A deposit passes **four successive control levels**, each with its own message, its own
   failure mode, and its own blast radius: levels 1–3 can reject the **whole interchange**;
   levels 3–4 can reject a **whole deposit**; level 4 can reject **part** of one. A
   `226 Transfer complete` from the file transfer is an *acquittement technique* and nothing
   more. The filing exists when `INFENT CR` returns a **code bilan of VT**. → P5 §5.2, §5.4.

3. **The declaration and the payment are two separate verdicts on one deposit.**
   Volume II §2.7.2.3 is unambiguous: *"la partie déclarative et la partie paiement font l'objet
   de bilans distincts et doivent être analysés séparément"* — `AFL DEC`/`RFL DEC` is not
   `AFL TP`/`RFL TP`. The channel can and does accept the payment while rejecting the return, and
   in that case **the money has moved but nothing has been declared**. A reconciler with one
   success flag per deposit will report that case as success. → P5 §5.5.

4. **Correction is by substitution before a deadline, not by amendment.**
   Up to the **date limite de substitution (DLS)**, a later deposit *replaces* the earlier one
   entirely: earlier deposits become *"initiaux rectifiés"*, are treated as never having been
   filed, and vanish from the taxpayer's own portal view. After the DLS, nothing can be cancelled
   or replaced. And a replacement deposit that omits the payment data **cancels the previous
   payment**. This is a destructive-by-default correction model. → P4 §4.9.

5. **The channel is schedule-driven and the schedule is client-specific.**
   Monthly, quarterly or annual — determined by the *previous year's* tax — plus a franchise
   floor, plus a conditional liability test, plus a 60-day cession window. There is no single
   cadence to code. → P7 §7.1.

##### 1.6 Deposit granularity

| Level | Rule | Source |
|-------|------|--------|
| Interchange | One file from one partenaire EDI, containing **1 to n deposits** | Vol IV §4.1.1.4 |
| Functional group | Groups the `INFENT DP` messages; **signed as a unit** by `AUTACK` | Vol IV §4.1.1.4, A15 |
| Message (`INFENT DP`) | **One dossier only** = one `SIREN` + one `ROF` | Vol IV §4.1.1.4 |
| Deposit | = form(s) + télérèglement | Vol IV §4.4.10 |
| Form group (G4) | up to **99 999** occurrences | Vol IV §4.2.5.3 |

So a single interchange can carry many clients' filings, but **each client's filing is its own
message**, and the flux is identified per deposit by the `BGM` header (data 1001). Mixing flux
(TS with IS or CVAE) across messages inside one interchange is normal and expected.

##### 1.7 Two corrections to the dossier's own scope record

`scope_manifest.json` records `FR-DGFIP-003` as format **`XML`** and integration type
**`outbound`**. Both need refining, and this runbook proceeds on the corrected reading. The
filing, authority, frequency and conditionality are unchanged — this is a documentation
refinement, not a scope change.

| Manifest says | Reality per A1/A2/A4 | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| format **`XML`** | **UN/EDIFACT**, `INFENT DP` subset of `INFENT` D00B. There is no XML representation of this filing on the EDI channel at all. | Every payload artefact in P4 is EDIFACT. A build scoped from the manifest alone would produce the wrong serialiser entirely. |
| direction **`outbound`** | Outbound **and** inbound. Three distinct inbound message types carry the only proof the filing exists, and the return leg is where the substitution logic and the payment/declaration split are resolved. | `DIRECTIONS=both`, as this task was scoped. P5 is not optional scope. |

Similarly, the **landscape** entry lists *"EDI-TDFC: transmission of the annual liquidation
return 2502-SD"*. That is incorrect: Volume III-C §3.2.1 states that the **only** documents
admissible in an EDI-Paiement interchange for TS are `P-IDENTIF`, `2501` and `2502`, and that
both forms travel in `INFENT DP`. **Both TS forms ride EDI-Paiement; neither rides EDI-TDFC.**
The EDI-TDFC volumes were downloaded, checked and set aside — see `_source-manifest.md` §D.

##### 1.8 Confidence

**High for the interface, the payload and the control model; medium for the calendar.**

Everything above is taken from DGFiP's own published cahiers des charges, from the EDIFICAS
autotest structure tables that DGFiP's own conformity regime is built on, or from the CGI. The
field-level payload for millésime **2026** is established from `PAYE26D` (P4 §4.5), not inferred
from the older form spreadsheets.

Carried as unresolved:

- **A genuine deadline discrepancy.** Volume III-C states the 2502-SD deadline is *"toujours le
  31 janvier de l'année qui suit l'année d'imposition"*, with no exception. impots.gouv.fr's
  taxpayer guidance — and `scope_manifest.json` following it — gives **15 January** for the
  annual-filer case (< €4,000). Both are DGFiP publications and they disagree. The channel
  transmits an **échéance**, not a deadline, so this does not block the build; but it changes
  *when a job must run* and it must be resolved before go-live. → `effort-estimates.md` §6 Q1.
- **The accreditation path for a non-French provider** — no published remote alternative to the
  face-to-face appointment. → P2 §2.9, Q2.
- **Whether the legacy POSEIDON/FTPS endpoint or PassTrans is assigned** to a newly accredited
  partner: the current cahier des charges names POSEIDON, the current technical prerequisites
  name PassTrans. Assigned per-partner by DGFiP. → P3 §3.2, Q3.
- **No published rate limit, latency SLA or TLS CA list** beyond "2 working days" and the cipher
  preferences in A12/A13. → P3 §3.7, P7 §7.5.

None of these blocks structural design. All are enumerated with a resolution path in
`effort-estimates.md` §6.


### DGFIP-EDI / `P2-enrolment-access-credentials.md`

#### P2 — Enrolment, Access & Credentials
**FR · DGFIP-EDI · DGFiP — taxe sur les salaires via the EDI channel**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · 2 unresolved (both administrative, neither blocks build)
Source basis: Volume II Commun 2021 §2.2, §2.4; A15 *Pack technique nouvelle sécurisation EDI*;
A16 *Pack général*; A18 *Liste des partenaires EDI actifs*.

> **The decision in §2.1 must be taken before any code is written.** It determines whether this
> integration is a 6-month programme or a 6-week one, and it is not reversible cheaply.

---

##### 2.1 The operating-model decision

There are exactly two lawful ways to put a file into this channel (Vol II §2.2.1.2.1). There is
no third.

| | **Model A — become a partenaire EDI** | **Model B — contract an accredited partner** |
|---|---|---|
| Who transmits | Mercans, under its own DGFiP habilitation number | A third party from the **349-entry** public list (A18) |
| DGFiP relationship | Direct: convention, accreditation, ESI support | None — Mercans is the partner's customer |
| What Mercans builds | **Everything in P3–P7**: EDIFACT writer, AUTACK signer, transport client, return parser | The partner's own file interface — **out of this runbook's scope** |
| Accreditation lead time | Unknown; includes a **physical appointment in France** (§2.3) | None |
| EDIFICAS conformity attestation | **Mandatory** on Mercans' emitting software (§2.5) | The partner's obligation, not Mercans' |
| Control over the return leg | Full — receives `INFENT CR` directly | Mediated; depends entirely on what the partner exposes |
| Recurring obligations | CNIL/GDPR, confidentiality, retention, notification duties (§2.7) | Commercial only |

**Model B is a lawful, complete answer.** Nothing in the regulation requires a payroll provider
to be a partenaire EDI, and 349 accredited partners already exist. There is also a third
non-integration answer: the **espace professionnel (EFI)** portal, which Volume II §2.1.1.1
treats as a fully equivalent filing channel — *"les deux filières EDI et EFI ne sont pas
exclusives l'une de l'autre : la mixité des dépôts est permise"* (Vol IV §4.1.2.1). For a client
base with a handful of TS-liable employers, manual EFI filing is not a compliance breach.

> **This runbook is written for Model A**, because that is the only model with an integration to
> specify. If Model B or EFI is chosen, P3–P7 collapse to a supplier-integration exercise and
> `effort-estimates.md` §4 gives the reduced figure.

##### 2.2 What the *employer* must do — and it is not nothing

A frequent and expensive misreading: because the adhesion dossier was abolished, people conclude
the client has no prerequisites. That is false for the **payment** leg.

| Step | Who | Required? | Source |
|------|-----|-----------|--------|
| Adhesion dossier to the EDI teleprocedure | employer | **Abolished since 1 Jan 2016.** Any transmission by a partenaire EDI is *opposable* to the identified user | Vol II §2.2.1.1, CGI 1649 quater B bis |
| Create an **espace professionnel** on impots.gouv.fr | employer | **Yes** — needed to hold bank details | Vol II §2.2.1.2.2 |
| Adhere to the online service **"Payer"** | employer | **Yes** — bank accounts must be registered, valid and B2B-compatible | Vol II §2.2.1.2.2 |
| Print, sign and send the **SEPA B2B mandate to its bank** | employer | **Yes — "impérativement", before any payment** of TS, and again on every new account | Vol II §2.2.1.2.2 |
| Mandate the partenaire EDI | employer | Yes, contractually | Vol II §2.2.1.2.1 |

**The SEPA B2B mandate is the real gate.** SEPA interentreprises requires the debtor's bank to
hold a signed mandate; without it the direct debit is refused *by the bank*, downstream of every
DGFiP control. This failure therefore appears **after** a `VT` code bilan, not before it, and it
is invisible to this channel. → P7 §7.4.

Note also: *"le redevable reste responsable de ses obligations fiscales. En cas de défaillance
du partenaire EDI, c'est le redevable qui fera l'objet des mises en demeure"* (Vol II
§2.2.1.2.1). Liability does not transfer with the transmission.

##### 2.3 Becoming a partenaire EDI — the habilitation dossier

Filed with the **correspondant téléprocédures of the DRFiP for the region of the applicant's
registered office** (Vol II §2.4.3.1.1). Contents, verbatim from the cahier des charges:

| # | Item | Note |
|---|------|------|
| 1 | **Extrait K-BIS** less than three months old | Presupposes a French registration |
| 2 | A **demande d'habilitation** | No prescribed form |
| 3 | An information sheet: contact details, nature of activity, **technical means (CFT, FTPS)**, which procedures (TDFC / TVA / **EDI-PAIEMENT**), and the type of service offered | The protocol is declared *here*, before build |
| 4 | The **convention "DGFiP – PARTENAIRE EDI"**, strictly identical to the model fixed by arrêté, pre-filled, **in two copies** | Reproduced at Vol II ch. 8 |
| 5 | An **attestation of tax compliance** — form **n° 3666** | Obtained from the applicant's own espace professionnel |
| 6 | A description of the intended activity (own account / exclusive beneficiaries / third parties) | Determines the scope granted |
| 7 | A **mandate** where the signatory is not the legal representative | |

Outcome: acceptance is notified by return of the convention signed by the regional director;
refusal by plain letter. A successful applicant receives a **partner identification number that
must be embedded in its files**. The service d'assistance directe aux téléprocédures then sends
the connection parameters (Vol II §2.4.3.1.2).

**The convention is valid for one year and renews by tacit reconduction.** One habilitation
covers EDI-TDFC, EDI-Requête, EDI-TVA and **EDI-PAIEMENT** together — but *not* EDI-IR, which
needs a separate convention because of the personal nature of the data.

> **No published SLA exists for any step of this process.** See `effort-estimates.md` §1.

##### 2.4 The electronic accreditation — the actual credential

This is not a certificate from a public CA and it cannot be requested online.

**Procedure (Vol II §2.4.3.2.1.2), in order:**

1. The partner **generates its own RSA key pair** to DGFiP's technical prescriptions.
2. It contacts its regional correspondent to **book an appointment**.
3. It **emails the public-key file in advance** so the correspondent can verify its technical
   conformity.
4. After confirmation, the partner's representative **attends in person**, carrying:
   - the validated public key **on a USB stick**;
   - a mandate, if not the legal representative;
   - a copy of a valid identity document;
   - a copy of the signed DGFiP/partner convention;
   - a K-bis less than three months old.
5. The correspondent **generates the accreditation on the spot and writes it to the partner's
   USB stick**.
6. The representative leaves with an accreditation *"immédiatement utilisable"*.

**Key parameters, from the runnable example in A15:**

| Parameter | Value | Evidence |
|-----------|-------|----------|
| Algorithm | **RSA** | `openssl genrsa` |
| Key length | **2048 bits** | `openssl genrsa -des3 -rand $0 -out cles_partenaire.pem 2048` |
| Private key storage | PEM, **password-protected (`-des3`)** | same |
| Public key export | PEM, `openssl rsa -pubout` | `1_exportation_cle_publique/commande_executee.sh` |
| Accreditation artefact | a **1368-character** hex file | `2_creation_accreditation_par_DGFiP/lisezmoi.txt` |
| Accreditation contents | SIRET ‖ partner number ‖ filler ‖ DER-encoded public key ‖ **DGFiP's signature over it** | decode of `accreditation_pedi.txt` |
| Sample identity | fictitious partner, SIRET `07955542100019` | `lisezmoi.txt` |

**Compromise procedure:** generate a new key pair and repeat the face-to-face procedure; the ESI
deactivates the old accreditation (Vol II §2.4.3.2.1.3). There is **no online revocation and no
rotation schedule** — meaning an emergency key rotation costs another trip to France. Treat the
private key accordingly: HSM or equivalent, and rehearse the loss scenario before go-live
(P7 §7.6).

One accreditation covers **all** EDI teleprocedures the partner is habilitated for
(§2.4.3.2.1.3) — so TS shares a credential with TVA, TDFC and IS. Blast radius of a compromise
is the whole DGFiP relationship, not one tax.

##### 2.5 EDIFICAS conformity attestation — a second, separate gate

Distinct from habilitation and from accreditation, and frequently missed.

- The partner **must** use software that has obtained the EDIFICAS conformity attestation
  (Vol II §2.4.3.3.2, and art. 3 of the convention type). This is an obligation on Mercans'
  *own* emitting module if Model A is chosen.
- Every emitted message must carry, in `RFF`: **editor name, program name, version number,
  revision number, and the conformity attestation reference** (Vol II §2.4.3.3.2). These are
  `RFF+AUM`, `RFF+AUN`, `RFF+AUO` — visible in the signed sample at A15
  `groupe_fonctionnel_a_signer.txt`. → P4 §4.4.
- Scope of the attestation is deliberately narrow: it covers **EDIFACT syntactic conformity of
  the emitting module only** — not the accounting application, not the content of the exchange
  (Vol II §2.4.3.3, "IMPORTANT" box). Passing it does not mean the filing is correct.
- Process: apply to EDIFICAS, receive a self-test dossier plus test data and a self-evaluation
  tool, return results, EDIFICAS issues a test report and the attestation.
- Administered by **EDIFICAS, 19 rue Cognacq-Jay, 75341 Paris Cedex 07**; rules and the list of
  attested products at `edificas.org`.

The self-test material is public and already in hand: A17 (`tables_population_fictive_pedi_v9.7`)
and the 2026 autotest structure tables `PAYE26D`/`PAYE26F` (A24). → P6 §6.3.

##### 2.6 Transmission parameters — issued, never chosen

| Parameter | How obtained | Source |
|-----------|--------------|--------|
| Partner identification number | Assigned at habilitation; **must be embedded in the files** | Vol II §2.4.3.1.2 |
| Connection parameters | Sent by the service d'assistance directe aux téléprocédures **after** habilitation | Vol II §2.4.3.1.2, §2.5.2 |
| Deposit and withdrawal directories | **Specific to each partner**; creation and change are **forbidden** | Vol II §2.5.2, A14 |
| FTPS/SFTP user id and password | Communicated individually | A14 |
| Protocol (one only) | Declared in the habilitation dossier, agreed with the ESI | Vol II §2.4.3.1.1, §2.5.2 |

Nothing here is self-service. Every value is provisioned by DGFiP after a human decision, which
is why P6's connectivity tests cannot start until §2.3 and §2.4 have completed.

##### 2.7 Standing obligations once accredited

These are conditions of keeping the habilitation, and they are ongoing (Vol II §2.4.4, and the
authority's *Les obligations des partenaires EDI* page).

| Obligation | Content |
|------------|---------|
| Inform the taxpayer | The taxpayer may query its status with the mandated partner at any time; the partner must disclose use of a subcontractor, and must notify all clients on ceasing activity or losing accreditation |
| CNIL / data protection | Declare the automated processing under Law 78-17 as amended |
| Confidentiality | Transmitted fiscal and accounting data may not be disclosed, transferred or reproduced to anyone other than another partenaire EDI under contract, or DGFiP. Any wider sharing requires **express taxpayer authorisation** |
| Retention | Retain transmitted data **only as long as necessary** for transmission and confirmation of receipt; longer retention needs the taxpayer's consent |
| Subcontracting | Permitted — a partner facing technical problems must use an approved subcontractor (Vol II §2.6.4.2) — but clients must be informed |

Note the tension the design must resolve: DGFiP's retention rule says *keep it only as long as
necessary*, while the evidentiary rule (P5 §5.7) requires keeping the `INFENT CR` as proof of
filing. These are reconcilable — the **processing report** is the client's evidence and is
retained on the client's behalf; the **raw payload** is not retained beyond need. Make that
distinction explicit in the retention policy. → P7 §7.6.

##### 2.8 Support routing

| Channel | Address | Use for |
|---------|---------|---------|
| Service d'assistance directe aux téléprocédures | via the regional correspondent | Interchange tracking, processing/rejection queries, connection parameters. **Explicitly not** a development-support service, and it does **not** answer the partner's own clients (Vol II §2.4.3.1.2 note 9) |
| EDI assistance | `assistance-edi@dgfip.finances.gouv.fr` | Always in copy on transfer incidents (A12 §3) |
| PassTrans transfer incidents | `esi.chalons.transferts@dgfip.finances.gouv.fr` | **Technical transfer problems only**, copying the two addresses above (A12 §3) |
| PassTrans platform | `passtrans.contact@dgfip.finances.gouv.fr` | In copy on transfer incidents |
| Operating establishment | **ESI de Strasbourg Neudorf, BP 80220** | Operates the EDI-Paiement files (Vol IV §4.5.5) |
| Regional correspondents | impots.gouv.fr → Contact > Professionnel > Vos correspondants spécialisés > Correspondants téléprocédures | Habilitation, accreditation appointments, relocation |
| Taxpayer-side queries | The client's **SIE** | Situation queries, derogations in exceptional urgency (Vol II §2.6.4.1) |

The partner is required to be able to supply its **transfer logs and the associated commands**
on request; without them DGFiP states incident analysis may be impossible (A12 §3). Log
retention is therefore an operational requirement, not a nicety. → P7 §7.5.

##### 2.9 Open items

| # | Question | Why it matters | Route |
|---|----------|----------------|-------|
| **Q2** | Is there any remote alternative to the **face-to-face** accreditation appointment for an applicant without a French establishment — and does the K-BIS requirement effectively mandate a French legal entity? | Determines whether Model A is available to Mercans at all | Regional correspondent, before any build commitment |
| **Q4** | What is the actual elapsed time from habilitation dossier to usable accreditation? | Sole determinant of the critical path | Same question, same call |

Both are **administrative**, not technical: development against the specification can proceed
in full while they are open, and only P6's live testing is gated. Ask them in the *first*
contact, together, and record the answers here.


### DGFIP-EDI / `P3-transport-interface-contract.md`

#### P3 — Transport & Interface Contract
**FR · DGFIP-EDI · DGFiP — taxe sur les salaires via the EDI channel**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · 3 unresolved (all provisioning-dependent)
Source basis: A12 PassTrans SFTP v1.1 (04/06/2026), A13 PassTrans PESIT v1.1 (04/06/2026),
A14 POSEIDON FTPS v5.1, Volume II §2.5–2.7, Volume IV §4.1, §4.3.

Every row below is tagged **[CURRENT]** (A12/A13, dated 2026), **[LEGACY]** (A14/Vol II, the
POSEIDON generation), or **[PROVISIONED]** (value issued by DGFiP per partner, not published).

---

##### 3.1 There is no request/response interface

This is worth stating plainly because it inverts the assumptions of every other integration in
the FR dossier. There is **no endpoint to call, no synchronous reply, and no correlation id you
choose**. The interface is:

```
  write a file into a directory you were assigned
      ↓  (0–2 working days)
  read files out of a different directory you were assigned
```

Everything else — identity, correlation, acceptance, error reporting — lives **inside the file
content**, not in a protocol envelope. Design consequence: the "client" here is a scheduled
file mover plus a message parser, and all the intelligence sits in P4 and P5.

##### 3.2 Transport platforms — two generations, both documented

The current cahier des charges (Volume II §2.5.1, 2021) names **POSEIDON** and the protocols
**CFT** and **FTPS**. The current technical prerequisites (A12/A13, **dated 04/06/2026**) name
**PassTrans** and the protocols **SFTP** and **PESIT**. Both are live DGFiP publications and
they describe different platforms.

| | **PassTrans** **[CURRENT]** | **POSEIDON** **[LEGACY]** |
|---|---|---|
| Product | Axway **Secure Transport (ST)** | Axway (XFB family) |
| Protocols | **SFTP**, **PESIT** (incl. PESIT over TLS) | **FTPS** (implicit SSL), **CFT/IP** |
| Documented in | A12, A13 — v1.1, 04/06/2026 | A14 v5.1; Vol II §2.5 |
| Host | **[PROVISIONED]** | `poseidon2.impots.gouv.fr` (`145.242.11.75`) |
| Ports | **[PROVISIONED]** | **2221** deposit, **2222** withdrawal; data **50000–50999** |
| Max transfer | **4 GB** | not stated |
| Status signal | A14 itself announces "POSEIDON migrera en 2020 vers une nouvelle solution" | superseded in practice |

> **Q3 — unresolved.** Which platform a newly accredited partner is assigned is **not published**
> and is decided by DGFiP when it issues connection parameters (Vol II §2.5.2). **Do not hard-code
> either.** Build the transport layer behind an interface with two implementations, or defer the
> transport implementation until the parameters arrive; the message layer (P4/P5) is identical
> either way. Ask the question in the same first contact as P2 Q2/Q4.

**The protocol is declared in the habilitation dossier** (P2 §2.3 item 3) and Volume II §2.5.2
forbids using more than one: *"celui-ci ne peut exploiter qu'un seul mode de transmission (soit
CFT, soit FTPS)"*, and *"les partenaires EDI utiliseront le même protocole pour les flux aller et
retour"*. **One protocol, both directions.** This is a technical-architecture decision taken at
application time, before any code exists.

##### 3.3 Connection parameters

###### PassTrans / SFTP **[CURRENT]** — A12

| Parameter | Requirement |
|-----------|-------------|
| SSH version | **SSH-2 only** |
| Ciphers | AES in **CTR** or **GCM** |
| Key exchange | **ECDH**, or DH with SHA-2 (e.g. `diffie-hellman-group-exchange-sha256`) |
| MAC | `hmac-sha2-256`, `hmac-sha2-512` |
| Consequence of stale algorithms | *"avec une version trop ancienne, vos transferts seront bloqués"* |
| Client software | Must be a **vendor-supported version** with an active support contract |

###### PassTrans / PESIT **[CURRENT]** — A13

| Parameter | Requirement |
|-----------|-------------|
| TLS | **v1.2 minimum** for any over-TLS protocol (PESIT TLS, HTTP TLS, FTP over TLS) |
| Preferred ciphers | `TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_128_GCM_SHA256`, `TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_256_GCM_SHA384`, `TLS_ECDHE_ECDSA_WITH_AES_128_GCM_SHA256` |
| Axway note | **XFB Gateway is end-of-life**; last supported version 6.17.3. A support matrix ships as the annexe to A13 |

###### POSEIDON / FTPS **[LEGACY]** — A14

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Host | `poseidon2.impots.gouv.fr` (`145.242.11.75`) |
| Encryption | **Implicit** SSL/TLS (FileZilla: "Connexion FTP implicite sur TLS") |
| Ports | **2221** deposit · **2222** withdrawal · data channel **50000–50999** |
| Mode | **Passive** |
| Filename encoding | **UTF-8** (must be forced) |
| Credentials | Username + password, **[PROVISIONED]** |
| Firewall | Outbound allow to `145.242.11.75` on 2221, 2222 and 50000–50999 |

> **No live endpoint was contacted during this research.** Every value above is read from the
> published guide. Verify against the parameters DGFiP actually issues before use.

##### 3.4 Directory contract — the rules that break naive clients

Identical in substance across both generations, and unusually restrictive:

| Rule | Platform | Source |
|------|----------|--------|
| Deposit and withdrawal use **separate directories** | both | A14, Vol II §2.5.2 |
| Directories are **specific to each partner** and **shared across all EDI teleprocedures** — TS, TVA, TDFC and IS land in the same place | both | Vol II §2.5.2 |
| **Creating or changing a directory is forbidden** | both | A14, Vol II §2.5.2 |
| **Overwriting a previously deposited file is forbidden** — filenames must be unique | POSEIDON | A14 |
| **Do not deposit under a temporary name and rename** | PassTrans SFTP | A12 §3 |
| **Do not open a file merely to check it exists** and close it without downloading | PassTrans SFTP | A12 §3 |
| **Do not delete files** you have retrieved | PassTrans | A12 §3 |
| **Do not open one session per file** — one session, n files | both PassTrans protocols | A12 §3, A13 §3 |
| **Do not poll unreasonably often** | PassTrans SFTP | A12 §3 |

Because the directories are shared across teleprocedures, **the retrieval loop must filter by
message type from file content, not assume everything in the directory is TS.** → P5 §5.3.

###### Retention on the platform **[LEGACY figures]**

| | Retention |
|---|---|
| Files you deposit | removed from your deposit directory at **J+2 maximum** |
| Files made available to you | removed **on download**, or after **N days — default 21** |

Two consequences: (a) **download is destructive** — A14 warns that a FileZilla re-download can
overwrite the local copy with an empty file; persist to durable storage immediately on retrieval
and never rely on the server as an archive; (b) a retrieval outage longer than ~21 days loses
returns permanently, and the only recovery is the DGFiP *rejeu* procedure (P5 §5.7). → P7 §7.5.

##### 3.5 Timing semantics — what counts as the filing date

| Mode | Filing date is | Source |
|------|----------------|--------|
| **Synchronous / direct transfer (CFT)** | the date of the **session** | Vol II §2.6.2.2 |
| **Asynchronous / indirect transfer (FTPS)** | the date the file is **placed in the DGFiP directory** | Vol II §2.6.2.3 |

Technical acknowledgement of the deposit is the FTP reply **`226 Transfer complete`** (A14
note 1): *"la prise en compte de votre fichier par la DGFiP est réalisée dès son dépôt, soit
après l'acquittement technique"*.

> **`226 Transfer complete` establishes the filing *date*. It says nothing about the filing
> being valid.** All four control levels are still ahead. Log it as the timestamp of record and
> as nothing else. → P5 §5.2.

**Service windows:** links operate in continuous emission/reception, *"tous les jours, samedi,
dimanche et jours fériés inclus"* (Vol II §2.5.2). There is no maintenance window published, and
no batch cut-off — but see §3.6 on when returns appear.

**No grace period exists.** Volume II §2.6.1: *"Aucun délai supplémentaire de dépôt, ni mesure de
tolérance en cas de rejets pour un motif technique, ne peuvent être accordés."* A technical
rejection at 23:50 on the deadline is a late filing. This is the single strongest argument for
the pre-flight gate in P4 §4.10 and for the schedule margin in P7 §7.1.

##### 3.6 Return retrieval

| Property | Value | Source |
|----------|-------|--------|
| Delivery SLA | **≤ 2 working days** from receipt at the ESI | Vol II §2.5.2, §2.7.2.1 |
| CFT partners | The partner's transfer program **must be active and listening** during the service periods | Vol II §2.5.2 |
| FTPS/SFTP partners | Retrieved from the **withdrawal directory** — a different directory from the deposit one | Vol II §2.5.2, A14 |
| Return media | **The same medium used to send** | Vol IV §4.1.1.2 |
| Signature | DGFiP's EDIFACT returns are **signed** (`AUTACK`), guaranteeing integrity and authenticating the sender | Vol II §2.7.2.3 |
| Responsibility | *"Il incombe au partenaire EDI de s'assurer de la bonne réception des données par la DGFiP ou des éventuels incidents de transfert"* | Vol II §2.5.2 |

An `INFENT RCS` acknowledging receipt is emitted **systematically, before any processing**, for
network transmissions (Vol IV §4.1.1.2). So the absence of an RCS is itself a signal. → P5 §5.2.

##### 3.7 Securing — the layer that is not optional

Applied to **all** declarative information transmitted in EDIFACT format, mandatorily
(Vol II §2.4.3.2.1.1). *"Tout envoi non sécurisé électroniquement sera rejeté dans sa globalité"*
(Vol II §2.7.2.3).

| Property | Value | Source |
|----------|-------|--------|
| Unit of signature | The **functional group** (`UNG…UNE`) containing the `INFENT DP` messages | A15, Vol IV §4.1.1.4 |
| Carrier | An `AUTACK` message in its **own functional group**, alongside the `INFENT DP` group, both inside one interchange | Vol IV §4.1.1.4 |
| Digest | **SHA-256** over the functional group | A15 `signer_donnees.c` |
| Signed block | `SHA256(GF)` ‖ **date/heure** — the timestamp is inside the signature | A15 `LG_HASH_A_SIGNER = SHA256_DIGEST_LENGTH + LG_DATE_HEURE` |
| Signature | `RSA_private_encrypt(..., RSA_PKCS1_PADDING)` with the 2048-bit private key | A15 |
| Certificate placement | Digital certificate in **`0560/USR/G2`** | Vol IV §4.1.1.4 |
| Seal placement | Secure seal in **`0560/USY/G3`** | Vol IV §4.1.1.4 |
| In-message security segments | Security group 1 (`USH`+`USA`), group 2 (`USC`+`USA`+`USR`), group 3 (`UST`+`USR`) | Vol IV §4.2.5.3 |
| Payload encryption | **None** — files are not encrypted; confidentiality is the transport's job | Vol II §2.7.2.3 |
| What it delivers | Authentication of the sender, integrity of the data, and **non-repudiation** | Vol II §2.4.3.2.1.1 |

A15 ships this as a **runnable worked example** — `signer_donnees.c`, `compilation.sh`, a sample
key pair, a sample accreditation, and a real signed functional group — plus two *separate*
verification directories, `4_verifier_ancienne_signature_infent_cr_rcs` and
`4_verifier_nouvelle_signature_infent_cr_rcs`. **The build must be able to verify both the old
and the new DGFiP return-signature schemes**, because the pack still ships both. Treat
signature-verification as two code paths until DGFiP confirms the old one is retired.

> Tested by DGFiP under Linux with OpenSSL 0.9.8e (CLI) and 1.0.0g (library) — A15
> `infos_environnement.txt`. Those versions are long unsupported; the *algorithms* (RSA-2048,
> SHA-256, PKCS#1 v1.5) are implementable on any current library, and should be. Do not ship the
> bundled OpenSSL 1.0.0g source that comes inside A15.

##### 3.8 Test vs real mode is set in the transport envelope

Not a separate host, not a separate directory — **two fields inside the interchange**:

| Mode | `UNB` data **0035** | `UNG` data **0040** |
|------|--------------------|---------------------|
| **REAL** | not served | **`DP1`** |
| **TEST** | **`1`** (test d'échange) | **`DP3`** (`INF_DP_ST_IT` — secured messages, test security, test integration) |

*"Ces 2 valeurs sont obligatoires. Si l'une des 2 est absente, alors l'interchange sera rejeté
syntaxiquement"* (Vol IV §4.5). A mismatch between them is not a warning — it is an interchange
rejection. And the failure mode that matters commercially is the inverse: **a test file emitted
with `DP1` is a real filing.** → P6 §6.2.

##### 3.9 Failure and retry semantics

| Condition | Correct response | Source |
|-----------|------------------|--------|
| Transfer failed / uncertain | *"il lui appartient de réitérer son transfert pendant la plage réservée"* — retransmit | Vol II §2.5.2 |
| Level 1–2 rejection | **Whole interchange** rejected; resend **the entire interchange** after correction | Vol IV §4.4.10.1–2 |
| Level 3 service-segment error | Whole interchange rejected (treated as a level-2 rejection) | Vol IV §4.4.10.3 |
| Level 3 message-structure error | Only the erroneous `INFENT DP` message(s) rejected; resend **those messages** | Vol IV §4.4.10.3 |
| Level 4 rejection | Per code bilan — global or partial; see P5 §5.4 | Vol IV §4.4.10.4 |
| Partner has persistent technical problems | *"il lui appartient de recourir à un sous-traitant agréé"* — use an approved subcontractor | Vol II §2.6.4.2 |
| Deadline passed after a rejection | No tolerance; late-filing penalties (CGI 1727–1730) apply | Vol II §2.6.3.2 |

**Retry is at interchange or message granularity, never at "the failed record".** There is no
partial-resend of a form. Build the resend path to reconstruct and re-sign a whole interchange
from persisted source data — which means the source data must be persisted, not just the emitted
file. → P7 §7.3.

**Idempotency has no protocol support.** Filenames must be unique, overwrite is forbidden, and
re-sending the same content before the DLS is not an error — it is a **substitution** that
replaces the previous deposit (P4 §4.9). An accidental double-send is therefore usually harmless
*before* the DLS and harmful *after* it. Guard the send path with a state machine, not with a
uniqueness constraint on content.

##### 3.10 Not published

| Item | Status | Impact |
|------|--------|--------|
| PassTrans host, ports, credentials | **[PROVISIONED]** — Q3 | Transport layer deferred, not blocked |
| Rate limits, concurrent-session limits | Not published; only "don't open one session per file" and "don't poll unreasonably" (A12 §3) | Adopt a conservative fixed schedule (P7 §7.2) rather than aggressive polling |
| TLS/SSH trust anchors and CA list | Not published; only cipher preferences | Pin after first successful connection; raise with the ESI at parameter issue |
| Latency between deposit and each return type | Only the **2 working day** outer bound | Alert on the bound, not on an assumed typical (P7 §7.5) |
| Whether the old return-signature scheme is retired | Both verifiers still shipped in A15 | Implement both (§3.7) |


### DGFIP-EDI / `P4-outbound-payload-infent-dp.md`

#### P4 — Outbound Payload: the `INFENT DP` message
**FR · DGFIP-EDI · DGFiP — taxe sur les salaires via the EDI channel**

Status: **BUILD-READY** · field inventory is **millésime 2026-current**
Source basis: Volume IV §4.2 (GUM INFENT DP), Volume III 2021 + **Volume III 2025-12 v1.0**,
Volume III-C §3.1–3.6, the **`PAYE26D` / `PAYE26F` autotest structure tables (millésime 2026)**,
the 2501/2502 form specifications, and the signed sample interchange in A15.

> **Scope boundary.** This phase specifies **where each value goes and what shape it must take**.
> It does **not** specify how the amounts are computed — rates, bases, franchise, décote and
> abattement thresholds are CCG and Wage-Type-Catalogue scope. Where a threshold is named below
> it is quoted only to identify a field, never as an authority for the figure.
> → `INTERLINKAGES.md` §5.

---

##### 4.1 Message architecture

```
Interchange  UNB ......................................... UNZ
 ├── Functional group  UNG (0040=DP1|DP3) ............ UNE     ← signed as a unit
 │     ├── INFENT DP   message 1   (one SIREN + one ROF)
 │     ├── INFENT DP   message 2   (a different dossier)
 │     └── …                                                    1..n deposits
 └── Functional group  UNG (AUTACK) .................. UNE
       └── AUTACK      (certificate 0560/USR/G2, seal 0560/USY/G3)
```

- **One `INFENT DP` message = one dossier = one `SIREN` + one `ROF`** (Vol IV §4.1.1.4). A message
  never spans two taxpayers and never spans two fiscal obligations.
- **One interchange = 1..n deposits**, and may mix flux (TS with IS, CVAE, RCM…). The flux is
  identified per deposit in the `BGM` header, data **1001** (Vol IV §4.1.1.4, §3.1.2.1).
- Directory `D00B`; `INFENT DP` is built identically to the `INFENT DF` (EDI-TDFC) and
  `INFENT DT` (EDI-TVA) subsets (Vol III-C §3.2.1).

###### Segment table (Vol IV §4.2.5.3)

| Segment / group | Status | Max rep |
|---|---|---|
| `UNH` | M | 1 |
| **Security group 1** — `USH` (M), `USA` (R) | D | 1 |
| **Security group 2** — `USC` (M), `USA` (D×3), `USR` (R) | R | 1 |
| `BGM` Beginning of message | **M** | 1 |
| `DTM` Date/time | **M** | 1 |
| `RFF` Reference | R | **3** |
| **Group 1** — `NAD` (M), `RFF` (D) | **M** | **4** |
| **Group 4** — `SEQ` (M), **`IND` (M)**, `RFF`, `MOA`, `FTX`, `DTM`, `QTY`, `PCD`, `FII` (all D) | **M** | **99 999** |
| **Group 5** — `NAD` (M) → **Group 6** — `CTA` (M), `COM` (D×2) | D | 1 |
| **Group 7** — `CCI` (M), `CAV` (R) | D | 1 |
| **Security group 3** — `UST` (M), `USR` (R) | D | 1 |
| `UNT` | M | 1 |
