> from `PAYE26D`, and DGFiP ships a runnable worked example of the hardest part (the signing
> chain). What is **not** confident is the **access timeline**, which is entirely outside
> Mercans' control and has no published SLA anywhere.

---

##### 1. The critical path is accreditation, not code

| Gate | Owner | Published SLA | Est. elapsed |
|------|-------|---------------|--------------|
| Assemble the habilitation dossier (K-BIS, convention ×2, form 3666, activity statement) | Mercans + legal | n/a | 1–2 weeks |
| **DRFiP correspondent instructs the dossier → convention returned signed** | **DGFiP** | **None** | **Unknown — 4–12 weeks assumed** |
| Partner number issued; connection parameters sent by the assistance service | DGFiP | None | Days |
| Generate RSA-2048 key pair; email public key for conformity check | Mercans | n/a | 1 day |
| **Book and attend the face-to-face accreditation appointment in France** | **DGFiP + Mercans** | **None** | **Unknown — appointment-dependent** |
| **EDIFICAS conformity attestation** (request dossier → self-test → report → attestation) | **EDIFICAS** | **None** | **Unknown — 3–8 weeks assumed** |
| Client-side: espace professionnel + service "Payer" + **signed SEPA B2B mandate at the bank** | pilot client + its bank | None | 2–4 weeks, client-paced |

> The elapsed figures are **assumptions, not sourced facts.** Nothing DGFiP or EDIFICAS publishes
> commits to a turnaround for any of these steps. They are wider than the URSSAF-DPAE assumptions
> because each is a human decision by a different body, and one of them requires physical
> attendance.

**Two of these gates are unlike anything else in the FR dossier:**

- The **face-to-face appointment** (P2 §2.4) has no published remote alternative, and the K-BIS
  requirement presupposes a French registration. **This may make Model A unavailable to Mercans
  entirely** — question **Q2** in §6, and it should be asked before any build commitment.
- The **EDIFICAS attestation** (P2 §2.5) is a second, independent gate run by a different
  organisation, and it is mandatory. It is frequently missed in planning because it is not a
  DGFiP process.

**Development is not blocked by any of this.** The full specification is in hand; P4 and P5 can be
built and unit-tested offline, and the signing chain can be proven against DGFiP's own worked
example (P6 §6.2 gate 6) without any credential. **Only live testing is gated** — and note that
live testing here means *production in test mode* (P6 §6.1), so it is gated on accreditation, not
on a sandbox.

**Start the enrolment on day one, in parallel with everything else, and put Q1–Q5 in the first
contact.**

##### 2. Build effort by phase

| Phase | Work | Days |
|-------|------|-----:|
| **P1** | Understand the authority, the two forms, the conditional liability, the rhythms and the four-level control model (this runbook substitutes for most discovery) | **1** |
| **P2** | Credential handling: RSA-2048 key custody (HSM or equivalent), accreditation file loading, partner-number injection, EDIFICAS attestation reference in `RFF+AUO` | **3** |
| **P3** | Transport: one protocol behind a two-implementation interface (PassTrans SFTP/PESIT **or** POSEIDON FTPS), directory contract, unique filenames, download-then-persist, session discipline, `226` timestamp capture | **7** |
| **P3s** | **Securing**: EDIFACT `AUTACK` construction, RSA-2048 over SHA-256(GF)‖timestamp with PKCS#1, certificate in `0560/USR/G2`, seal in `0560/USY/G3`, **plus both** inbound signature verifiers | **8** |
| **P4** | **`INFENT DP` writer**: interchange/GF/message envelopes, the 25-char `IND` codification with mandatory padding, `P-IDENTIF` incl. `NAD+DT`, 2501 (3 fields), 2502 (**76 fields**, 5-column grid with the `EL` exception), payment block (FII BIC+IBAN, MOA ×3, RFF references), échéance derivation, **35-assertion pre-flight gate driven from `PAYE26D`** | **14** |
| **P5** | **Return parsing**: `INFENT RCS`, `CONTRL`, `INFENT CR`; signature verification; code bilan + **separate `DEC`/`TP` lot verdicts**; error catalogue incl. `CCDDDD`/`FFFFFFFFFFIIII` location decoding; per-deposit reconciliation; the substitution chain | **11** |
| **P6** | Test harness: 6 connectivity + 3 securing + 3 syntax + **14 business** + 5 return-parsing + 3 substitution cases against the fictitious population; EDIFICAS self-test; pilot cutover | **9** |
| **P7** | Derived scheduler (liability gate, franchise gate, rhythm from last year's tax, no-January rule, cession window), 6 jobs, **DLS-aware state machine**, alerting incl. the do-not-alert set, dual-store archival, palier refresh | **8** |
| | **Build subtotal** | **61** |

###### Where the effort actually concentrates

Three items are **more than half the build** and each is genuinely harder than it looks:

- **P4 at 14 days** — not because the forms are large, but because the addressing scheme is
  unforgiving. A 25-character index with mandatory space padding, a form code that must not be
  compressed, an `EL` that must not be generated, an `AD` that no longer exists, and a whole
  suppressed CITS block. All of it is mechanically checkable against `PAYE26D`, which is why the
  validator should be **data-driven from that file** rather than hand-written — that decision
  saves most of the annual maintenance cost in §5.
- **P3s at 8 days** — EDIFACT `AUTACK` is unfamiliar territory for most teams. The mitigating
  factor is unusually strong: A15 ships the C source, the commands, a key pair, an accreditation
  and a real signed functional group, so correctness is **provable offline before any
  transmission**. Budget the days; expect them to be low-risk.
- **P5 at 11 days** — the parsing is routine; the **state model is not**. Seven code-bilan values
  × two independent lot verdicts × a substitution chain that can retroactively annul an accepted
  deposit. Most of these days are the state machine and its tests, not the parser.

##### 3. Non-build effort

| Item | Owner | Days |
|------|-------|-----:|
| Habilitation dossier assembly, convention review, legal sign-off | Legal / compliance | 3 |
| Regional correspondent liaison, appointment logistics, travel | Ops | 2 + travel |
| EDIFICAS application and self-test administration | Dev + ops | 3 |
| Client-side prerequisite chase (espace pro, service "Payer", SEPA B2B mandate) per pilot client | Client services | 2 |
| Key custody design and the loss/compromise rehearsal | Security | 2 |
| Client-facing documentation (why deposits vanish after the DLS; why most clients file nothing) | Client services | 1 |
| | **Subtotal** | **13** |

##### 4. Totals, and the two cheaper options

| Option | Build | Non-build | **Total** | Elapsed |
|--------|------:|----------:|----------:|---------|
| **Model A — Mercans as partenaire EDI** | 61 | 13 | **74 person-days** | **6–9 months**, gated by accreditation + EDIFICAS |
| **Model B — contract an accredited partner** | ~12–18 | ~5 | **~17–23 person-days** | **4–8 weeks**, commercially gated |
| **EFI portal only — no integration** | 0 | ~2 | **~2 person-days** | Immediate |

**Model B** replaces P3, P3s and the transport half of P5 with one supplier interface, and
transfers habilitation, accreditation and EDIFICAS conformity to the partner. **P4's payload
knowledge is still needed** — the partner will want structured data, and the business coherence
rules (`400`–`409`) still have to be satisfied before submission — as is P7's derived schedule
and the liability gate. The range depends entirely on what the partner exposes; 349 accredited
partners exist (A18).

**EFI** is a fully lawful channel, expressly permitted to be mixed with EDI (P2 §2.1), and for a
client base with a handful of TS-liable employers it is a defensible answer indefinitely.

> **Recommendation.** Given that the TS-liable population is by definition a **minority** of a
> payroll book (P1 §1.3), and that Model A's critical path includes a physical appointment in
> France with no published SLA, **Model A is hard to justify on TS volume alone.** It becomes
> justifiable if Mercans also wants EDI-TVA or EDI-TDFC, since **one habilitation and one
> accreditation cover all three** (`INTERLINKAGES.md` §4). Take that decision explicitly, at
> portfolio level, before committing the 74 days — do not let it be decided by default.

##### 5. Recurring cost

| Item | Cadence | Days/yr |
|------|---------|--------:|
| **Palier refresh** — re-pull `PAYE26D`/`PAYE26F`, form-spec zip, Volume III-C; diff; re-point the validator; re-run the P6 T-series | **Annually, before December** | **4** |
| Transport prerequisite review (cipher/algorithm tightening — stale algorithms **block transfers**) | Annually | 1 |
| Convention renewal, correspondent notifications | Annually | 0.5 |
| EDIFICAS attestation reference maintenance on each software release | Per release | 0.5 |
| Run-and-operate: 6 jobs, exception handling, per-échéance runbook | Ongoing | ~6 |
| | | **~12/yr** |

The palier figure assumes the validator is **data-driven from `PAYE26D`**. If the field rules are
hand-coded instead, budget **10–12 days** a year and expect silent drift — the precedent is
concrete: the 2501 lost a field at millésime 2022 and the 2502 lost its entire CITS block, and
neither change announces itself except as a `Rejet RT`.

##### 6. Open questions, with routes

| # | Question | Blocks | Route |
|---|----------|--------|-------|
| **Q1** | **Is the 2502-SD deadline 15 or 31 January for an annual filer?** Volume III-C says *"toujours le 31 janvier"*; impots.gouv.fr taxpayer guidance says 15 January below €4,000. Both are DGFiP publications | Not the build — the **schedule**. J1/J2 timing (P7 §7.1) | SIE or the assistance service; also verifiable from a real client's own échéance |
| **Q2** | **Is there a remote alternative to the face-to-face accreditation appointment, and does the K-BIS requirement mandate a French legal entity?** | **Whether Model A is available at all** | Regional DRFiP correspondent — **ask before committing** |
| **Q3** | **PassTrans or POSEIDON?** Which platform, host, ports and directories are assigned to a new partner | The transport implementation only (P3 §3.2) | Service d'assistance directe, at parameter issue |
| **Q4** | Actual elapsed time: habilitation → accreditation; and EDIFICAS application → attestation | The critical path (§1) | Same first contact as Q2; EDIFICAS separately |
| **Q5** | The **numeric codes** for the generic level-4 anomalies — legible labels and dispositions were extracted, but the code numbers sit in `.doc` table cells that flattened (P5 §5.6) | Nothing — the gate is written against the rules, and the parser must tolerate unknown codes regardless | Open A4 in a word processor, or request the current Volume III from EDIFICAS as PDF |
| **Q6** | BIC/IBAN values for the fictitious test population — sheet 7 of A17 v9.7 is **not populated** | The **payment leg** of P6 only; everything else is testable | Assistance service, with the connection parameters |
| **Q7** | Is the **old** DGFiP return-signature scheme retired? A15 still ships both verifiers | Nothing — both are implemented (P3 §3.7) | Assistance service |

**None of Q1–Q7 blocks development.** Q2 is the only one that could change the answer to
"should we build this at all", and it is a phone call.

##### 7. What made this estimate possible

Worth recording, because it is unusually favourable and should not be assumed for the next
authority. Every specification DGFiP names as public was obtained without a login, a certificate
or a CAPTCHA — **no `[[NEEDS-ARTEFACT]]` marker is carried anywhere in this runbook**. In
particular:

- The **millésime-2026** payload is established from the EDIFICAS autotest structure tables
  (`PAYE26D`/`PAYE26F`), so P4 is current rather than reconstructed from the 2022 form
  spreadsheets — which would have been wrong in at least three places.
- DGFiP publishes a **runnable worked example** of the signing chain (A15), which converts the
  highest-risk component into a verifiable one.
- DGFiP publishes the **fictitious test population** (A17) with a cohort for each of the three TS
  filing rhythms, so P6's business cases have real identifiers to run against.

The one place the authority's own publication let us down — the 2022 "Volume III" link serving
Volume III-D's content — was worked around from the 2021 and 2026 editions and is recorded in
`_source-manifest.md`.
